# Decisions register: hard rules, thresholds and limits

Every hard decision taken on the way to a first working mesh is listed here: quality gates and their thresholds, rules
of the kind "cut here, do not cut there", limits and caps, and defaults that encode a choice. Each one is fixed for
now. It may change when FESOM3's mesh rules are known or when we try real meshes. A row says what the rule is, its
value, where it lives, why it has that value, how settled it is, and when to look at it again.

**Rule:** every new threshold, option or rule gets a row here in the same change (commit) that introduces it. A
changed value updates its row in the same change. A hard decision never lives only in code.

**Status:** *fixed* = settled by a decision, by a FESOM rule read in its code, or by a measurement we do not plan to
redo; *provisional* = our own choice or first guess, which works now and may be changed (update the row);
*open* = an open question: the value is in use but not decided yet ("revisit when" names the choice);
*exploration* = a value of exploration code (the mesh-fixing competition and similar trials): it works now and is not
hardened, see L13; *superseded* = replaced by a later row, kept for the record. "arbitrary, first guess" in "why"
means that no measurement or source stands behind the value; "design value, not calibrated" means the value was set
when meshom was designed and no measurement has tested it yet.

**References.** Code paths are relative to `meshom/`, so `config.py:39` means `meshom/config.py:39` and
`m14/cut.py` means `meshom/methods/m14/cut.py`. Line numbers date from the change that wrote or last updated a row and
may have moved since; the names (options, functions, constants) are the stable reference.

Abbreviations: config.md = `docs/config.md`; m14 note = `docs/methods/m14_blocks.md`; medi = four FESOM-C meshes of
the Mediterranean (v5_1, v9_1, v11_1 and a Gmsh version of v5_1), the first reference meshes.

---

## 1. Quality gates

`DEFAULT_GATES` (`config.py:38-50`) are evaluated by `check_gates` (`quality/metrics.py:190-217`). "Hard" means every
element; "target" means a percentile.

| # | rule | value now | where | why | status | revisit when |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| G0 | Every gate value is a seed until it is calibrated on FESOM-C meshes with noise labels | seeds | — | no record says which FESOM-C run was noisy, or where | open | noisy and good runs are labelled and the gates calibrated on them |
| G1 | Largest quad corner angle, every quad (hard) | ≤ 150° | `quad_max_angle_hard_deg`, config.py:39; metrics.py:191 | FESOM-C noise sat at large-angle elements | fixed | FESOM3 noise labels or FESOM3's own limit |
| G2 | Quad max angle at p99.9 (target) | ≤ 135° | config.py:40; metrics.py:192 | medi interior p99.9 133–134° on all four meshes | fixed | as G1 |
| G3 | Largest triangle angle, every triangle (hard) | ≤ 120° | config.py:41; metrics.py:193 | medi: only v5_1 fails, 4 triangles | fixed | FESOM3's rule for triangles is known |
| G4 | Triangle max angle at p99, triangle-only meshes (target) | ≤ 90°; skipped when the mesh has quads | config.py:42; metrics.py:194, 213-214 | design value, not calibrated | provisional | the first FESOM3 triangle-only run |
| G5 | Smallest triangle angle, every triangle (hard, non-degeneracy) | ≥ 20° | config.py:43; metrics.py:195 | arbitrary, first guess (medi minima 2.7–28.6°) | provisional | calibration labels (G0) |
| G6 | Quad scaled Jacobian = smallest corner sine, every quad (hard) | ≥ 0.3 (corners within 17.5°–162.5°) | config.py:44; metrics.py:196 | arbitrary, first guess | provisional | calibration labels (G0) |
| G7 | Element size ratio across each interior edge (mean edge length of the two elements), p99 (target) | ≤ 1.15 | config.py:45; metrics.py:197 | the design target: neighbours differ in size by 10–15 % at most | fixed | FESOM3 noise vs size jumps (medi p99 1.23–1.30) |
| G8 | Same ratio, max (hard) | ≤ 2.0 (no factor-2 jump) | config.py:46; metrics.py:198 | no 2:1 templates (a design anti-goal) | fixed | FESOM3 rules known |
| G9 | Size fidelity \|ln(s/h)\| per edge, p99 (target) | ≤ 0.15; a mesh built with a size tolerance t is judged at t (G38; the m14 methods: 0.20 by default) | config.py:47; metrics.py:164-184, 199 | JIGSAW production meshes sit at 0.100–0.146 | open | keep 0.15 per edge or loosen; measure per edge or per √area |
| G10 | Edge neighbours per element, min (hard) | ≥ 2 | config.py:48; metrics.py:200 | the FESOM2 mesh generator fesom2_mesh_gen (D. Sein, AWI) removes triangles with one neighbour; FESOM-C: 0 neighbours = STOP, < 2 only a warning; the medi meshes ran with 1, so ≥ 2 is our choice | fixed | FESOM3's rule is known |
| G11 | Elements per node, max (hard) | ≤ 32 | config.py:49; metrics.py:201 | the FESOM-C partitioner stops above 32 elements per node | fixed | FESOM3 partitioner limit known |
| G12 | Adjacent nodes per node, quad diagonals included | ≤ 32 needed, not measured, not a gate | — | the FESOM-C partitioner stops above 32 adjacent nodes | provisional | before the first FESOM-C / FESOM3 partitioner run: add the gate |
| G13 | No edge-connected cluster of ≥ 3 triangles outside triangle zones (hard) | `tri_cluster_max` = 2, declared triangle zones (`Mesh.tri_zone`) exempt, N/A without quads | recorded as `tri_clusters.n_ge3`, metrics.py:92-98; config.py `DEFAULT_GATES` | design rule: triangles outside declared zones stay isolated; the early m14_blocks meshes had 2 / 5 / 15 / 9 such clusters | fixed | FESOM3's tolerance to triangle strips is known |
| G14 | Boundary rule: no element with ≥ 2 boundary edges | option `boundary_rule` = `off` / `tri` / `all`, default `tri` (triangles only); gate `boundary_edges_max` = 1, applied per `boundary_rule` | recorded as `n_elems_ge2_boundary_edges`, metrics.py:103; config.py `DEFAULT_GATES` | soft: FESOM2's rule for triangles (a triangle with 2 boundary edges has one neighbour); quads at staircase corners allowed (616 in m14_blocks_cut continent s1); FESOM3 unknown | fixed | FESOM3's boundary rule is known (`all` if it rejects such quads) |
| G15 | Manifold boundary: every boundary node has exactly 2 boundary edges | `pinch_nodes_max` = 0 | recorded as `n_pinch_nodes`, metrics.py:102 | the boundary must be simple closed loops | fixed | — |
| G16 | Euler check: Σ_interior(4 − deg) + Σ_boundary(2 − elements) + T = 4χ, χ = 2 − loops | gate `euler_ok` (also a table column) | compete.py:122-139, 211-216 | a topology check: without it a broken channel scored 14/24 | fixed | — |
| G17 | Gates per subset: whole mesh, `_coast` (elements touching a boundary node, i.e. coastal row 1) and `_interior` | gate name + suffix | metrics.py:40-43, 209-216 | a global percentile must not hide a bad coast; medi shape tails sit in the coast row | fixed | — |
| G18 | Coastal rows 1–3 and strait corridors as subsets | `_coast3` (≤ 2 edge hops from the boundary), `_strait` (`cases.STRAITS`) | metrics.py:40-43 (subsets) | row 1 alone hides the coast's tail: m05 coast p99 0.097 over row 1, 0.158 over rows 1–3 | fixed | — |

| G20 | A gate without a value (no quads, no coast, no h) | passes and is not counted | metrics.py:187-189, 215; compete.py:217 | pinned by tests | fixed | — |
| G21 | Percentiles | `np.percentile`, linear interpolation | metrics.py:15 | arbitrary, first guess (the numpy default) | provisional | — |
| G22 | Triangle θmin at p0.1 (target) | ≥ 30°; not measured (the summary has p1 and min) | — | design value, not calibrated | provisional | when the summary gets p0.1 |
| G23 | Quad edge ratio at p99.9 (target) | ≤ 2; not measured (p99 and max only), not a gate | — | design value, not calibrated | provisional | the first anisotropic h |
| G24 | Triangle–triangle edges Delaunay on the sphere (hard) | not measured | — | design value, not measured | provisional | the first triangle method |
| G25 | n3, n5 and T each within ±30 % of ∫ρ_c⁺ and ∫ρ_c⁻ (target) | reported (columns ρ+ / ρ−), not a gate | compete.py:89-116 | arbitrary, first guess | provisional | after an aquaplanet test |
| G26 | Share of interior nodes with valence ≠ 6 in triangle meshes | reported, not a gate; aim ≤ 4 % (the JIGSAW level) | — | FESOM ran for years on CORE2 with 44 % | open | gate "≤ 5 %" or report only |
| G27 | Triangle share of a mixed mesh | gate `tri_share_max = 0.35` (triangles / elements; N/A for triangle-only meshes) | — | a large triangle zone is fine for now, better than bad quads; the level is a parameter | fixed | revisit with FESOM3's numerics on triangles |
| G28 | Node-based neighbour size ratio | reported only, not a gate | metrics.py:140-147 | fails 1.15 on every medi mesh because of valence and boundary effects | fixed | — |
| G29 | FV metrics (centroid-line non-orthogonality, sub-area sign), Shewchuk q∇, inradius/circumradius | not measured | — | FESOM-C is sensitive to them | provisional | before the first FESOM3 run |
| G30 | Elements flagged on the quality map | quads with SJ < 0.3 or above the hard angle; triangles above the hard angle | quality/report.py:58-62 | follows G1, G3, G6 | provisional | gates change |
| G31 | Repair of a quad that fails a hard gate: 2 triangles if both pass and no triangle chain forms, else remesh its cavity; all gates rerun | the 2-triangle split not built; cavity remeshing of quads > 135° and defect clusters: `meshom/repair.py` (§11), applied after a method (`*_rep`), not inside it | `meshom/repair.py` | design default | provisional | a mixed fill method |
| G32 | Seam gate: gross charge above 2 D_real per 10 corridor cells (D_real = the minimum for the lattices the mesh realised on the two sides, P26) | ≤ 2.0 (`seam_excess_per10_max`); the seam gates live in `SEAM_GATES` and are evaluated into the record's `seam` block only, never in `DEFAULT_GATES`: a separate seam table, not a column of the 4-case ranking | quality/seams.py `SEAM_GATES`, `seam_gates`; the `seam` block from compete.py `_score` (P29) | one surplus dislocation pair per 10 cells; the ideal ladder sits at 1.03× D; `_mix`'s strips at 2.1–2.6 irregular per 10 below 25° (m14 note); today m14 ≤ 0.1 (one block per lake), 2.1–2.6 at 45°, m02 ≤ 0.23 | provisional (a seed in the sense of G0) | calibration labels; the quantised joins |
| G33 | Seam gate: \|ln s/h\| p99 over the corridor's interior edges | ≤ 0.18 (`seam_ln_sh_p99_max`); a mesh built at the size tolerance t (G38, `methods.effective_tolerance`) is judged at max(0.18, t) | quality/seams.py `SEAM_GATES`, `tolerance_seam_gates`; compete.py `run_case`, `_score` (`tol`); cases_seams.py `rescore` | P22's seam-band level; G9's 0.15 stays on the whole mesh; an ideal dense wall at 45° reaches ≈ 0.15; m02's bend sits at 0.16–0.31; tolerance-aware as G38 (2026-10-24): `m14_blocks_rep` at 0.20 failed "size" on 12 of the 15 seam lakes for the gate alone; re-scored from the saved meshes: 3 → 8 of 15 pass every seam gate (the rest: centre, lake excess) | provisional (G0) | as G9; the choice between a wall and a bend |
| G34 | Seam gate: quad max angle p99.9 over the corridor's quads | ≤ 135° (`seam_quad_p99_9_max`) | quality/seams.py `SEAM_GATES` | G2 on the corridor | provisional (G0) | as G2 |
| G35 | Seam gate: element size ratio p99 across the corridor's interior edges | ≤ 1.15 (`seam_ratio_p99_max`) | quality/seams.py `SEAM_GATES` | G7 on the corridor; today m14 ≤ 1.144 (coast lake 1.168), m02 ≤ 1.20 | provisional (G0) | as G7; B33 |
| G36 | Seam gate: share of the corridor's irregular nodes within 1 h of the centreline | ≥ 0.5 (`seam_centre_share_min`), applied from 5 irregular nodes in the corridor (`CENTRE_MIN_IRREGULAR`) | quality/seams.py `SEAM_GATES`, `CENTRE_MIN_IRREGULAR`, `CENTRE_CELLS` | the dislocations belong on the mid-line, evenly spaced (clusters of 4: p99 0.30; the outline row doubles the maximum, 0.34–0.48); m14's real seams hold 71–78 % within 1 h | provisional (G0) | — |

| G38 | Size-fidelity gate at the size tolerance | `[mesh] size_tolerance` = t (or the method option `size_tolerance`, e.g. `meshom compete --opts`) sets `size_fidelity_p99` = t on the whole mesh and every subset, unless the config's `[gates]` names that gate. Default (none given): the method's, **0.20 for the m14 methods** (`m14_blocks` DEFAULTS, every variant; `m14_blocks_rep` takes its base method's), none for the others (m15_tri too); 0 = off (s = h, G9's 0.15). One effective tolerance per run (`methods.effective_tolerance`) is what the method builds at and what the gate judges at. The element-ratio gates G7, G8 never change | methods/__init__.py `effective_tolerance`; m14_blocks.py DEFAULTS; config.py `tolerance_gates`, `Config.gates_all`, `Config.size_tolerance`, `MeshCfg.size_tolerance`; compete.py `run_case` (a gate the caller passes in `gates` is kept, as a config's [gates]); run.py (the method's opts, the record's `opts`); the seam size gate G33 follows the same tolerance | h is often drawn by eye, the mesh need not follow it strictly; a parameter says how far the cell size may deviate from h and within it smoothness wins; a mesh is judged against the tolerance it was built for | fixed (the rule; the default 0.20 for the m14 methods, none for m15_tri, from CORE2-like `m14_blocks_rep` 39/39 against 31/39, ratio p99 1.206 → 1.110, every scale-1 gate of `_rep`; docs/methods/m14_blocks.md "Smoothness within a size tolerance") | — |

## 2. Size function and limiter

| # | rule | value now | where | why | status | revisit when |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S1 | h means the target edge length, in metres | — | size.py:3 | definition | fixed | — |
| S0 | Base h of a config | the largest constant layer, else `[size] h_max_m`, else the largest h_m / h_out_m of the enabled layers | `meshom/config.py` `Config.base_h` | the UI's h builder lets the user drop the constant layer (a zone-only config crashed the preview) | fixed | if a config wants a different base, set h_max_m |
| S2 | Assembly order | h = scale × limit(clip(the layers in file order (S34; min alone before 2026-10-26), h_min, h_max), grad_max) | run.py:80-99; config.md:108 | as in fesom2_mesh_gen (D. Sein, AWI), the FESOM2 mesh generator: read → refine → cap → clip → limit, the limit last | fixed | — |
| S3 | Gradient limit | `grad_max` = 0.1 (\|∇h\| ≤ 0.1: neighbours differ by ≤ 1 + g); `inf` = off | config.py:125; config.md:113 | the design target of 10–15 % between neighbours; continent case g = 0.1; FESOM2 production meshes of fesom2_mesh_gen use 0.07–0.15 | provisional | FESOM3 noise at transitions |
| S4 | Global clip | `h_min_m` / `h_max_m` optional, none by default; a hard clip | config.py:126-127; size.py:225-233 | the assembly order (S2) | fixed | — |
| S5 | Soft cap before the clip | not built (hard clip only) | — | fesom2_mesh_gen uses a soft cap because a hard clip gave a kink and a visible ring | provisional | a ring at h_max shows on a real mesh |
| S6 | Each criterion limited once and cached | not built: one limiter pass on the minimum | — | limiting distributes over min | provisional | criteria become expensive |
| S7 | Limiter algorithm | Dijkstra relaxation h_j ≤ h_i + g d_ij on a grid graph; Numba; deterministic | size.py:423-454 | Lipschitz limiting | fixed | — |
| S8 | Limiter grid | equiangular cubed sphere: no pole, no antimeridian, cell edges 0.71–1 × the nominal spacing, O(1) point location | size.py:236-244 | no pole, no antimeridian, O(1) point location; a block-adaptive cubed sphere is the alternative | provisional | h_min < ~14 km (the cap of S10 is hit) |
| S9 | Limiter grid spacing | half the smallest h the layers can take, × scale | run.py:69-77, 90 | a size feature ≥ 2 h_min then spans several cells | provisional | — |
| S10 | Limiter grid node cap | 12 000 000 nodes (≈ 1.1 GB of arrays, spacing ≥ 7.1 km; 26 s and 1.4 GB on 8 Levante cores) | `LIMITER_MAX_NODES`, size.py:246; `limiter_max_nodes`, config.py:129 | memory | provisional | configs with h_min < 14 km; a block-adaptive grid |
| S11 | Limiter stencil and the limited field's slope | 16 neighbours: graph distance ≤ 2.7 % longer than the geodesic in square cells, measured ≤ 5 % near a cube corner (60° cells, 3 faces at the corner node: 1.049 g at 4 cells) (8 neighbours: 8.2 %). The bound g holds exactly on every stencil edge; the returned bilinear field's slope is ≤ g × (1 + metrication) over two cells (= h_floor, the shortest mesh edge) and reaches up to 2 g pointwise within one cell of a kink of h (a ramp's start at a step, a cone apex, a ridge): the cell's gradient lies in the box of its edge differences, ≤ g √(2 / (1 − \|cos a\|)), √2 g in square cells, 2 g at 60° | size.py `_STENCIL`, `Limited`, `limit_gradient` | metrication error; the overshoot is that of any exact-at-node interpolant linear along the cell edges (a cone's nodes rise g L along two edges from its apex), so it is not removed by another such interpolant. Measured (2026-10-27; a first probe gave 1.69 g at a step zone's corner, at 2 m): step zone at a cube corner (cells 99 km), worst \|Δh\| / (g d) over d = 2 m, L/4, L/2, L, 2L, 4L: 1.65, 1.52, 1.35, 1.11, 1.050, 1.049; 1 000 km disc steps at (0,0), (180,0), (0,89): 1.38–1.40 at 2 m, 1.10–1.11 at L, 1.027–1.028 at 2L; fesom2_core2like (cells 12.5 km, the limiter lowers no node: the overshoot is the bilinear of the layers' own kinks): 1.41, 1.31, 1.24, 1.07, 1.015, 1.003; tests/test_run.py `test_limited_field_slope_by_scale`. A pointwise ≤ g with bilinear cells needs the nodes limited at g / 2 (cube corners; g / √2 in square cells): core2like's node estimate 243 606 → 276 073 (+13.3 %; +5.5 % at g / √2), transitions up to 2 × wider; a min-of-cones interpolant meets g exactly but scallops a constant h by g L / √2 (the smoothness requirement). Not changed for now | provisional | a pointwise bound is required, or a method reads \|∇h\| below the cell scale |
| S12 | Limiter domain | the whole sphere, across land | size.py:436-437 | as fesom2_mesh_gen; water plus a land margin would be better, but a land mask on 10⁷ nodes is too slow | provisional | the coast filter, or a strait refinement leaks through thin land |
| S13 | h between limiter nodes | bilinear interpolant in difference form (exact on a constant field) | size.py:393-407 | a constant field stays exact (the plain form gave 49 999.999 instead of 50 000) | fixed | — |
| S14 | Limiter spacing when none is given | half the minimum of h over 2·10⁵ Fibonacci points | size.py:439-440 | arbitrary, first guess (sample size) | provisional | — |
| S15 | Scale | the final h (after the limiter) × scale: nodes ∝ 1/scale², ρ_c unchanged, \|∇h\| × scale | config.md:82; run.py:94-95; size.py:100-102 | `meshom compete --scale`; scale 4 makes g = 0.4, so smoothness is judged at scale 1; fesom2_mesh_gen scales before cap, clip and limit | fixed | — |
| S16 | Base h | the largest `constant` layer, else `h_max_m`, else none | config.py:152-154; config.md:118-120 | coast_distance layers stop growing at it, zones grow to it | provisional | — |
| S17 | Layer `latitude` | h = max(h_m cos(lat), h_min_m) | size.py:157-164 | h ∝ cos φ, as on a regular lon-lat grid | provisional | — |
| S18 | Layer `coast_distance` | h = h_m + grad · max(d − plateau_m, 0), at most the base h; d to the coast vertices plus points every ≤ h_m/4 (3-D KD-tree) | size.py:167-196; run.py:58 | the search stops at the base h (UI preview speed); distance error ≤ h/8 | provisional | — |
| S19 | Layer `zone` | h_m inside a great-circle polygon, linear to h_out_m over transition_m; transition_m = 0 is a step that the limiter grades; the polygon must fit in a hemisphere (any open hemisphere: the inside test and the near-cap are centred on `polygon_centre`, the vertex mean when every vertex is in its hemisphere, else the centre of the smallest cap holding the vertices, by one NNLS) | size.py `polygon_centre`, `polygon_inside`, `_zone_near`, `Zone`; config.md:127 | the gnomonic inside test is exact only within a hemisphere. Before 2026-10-27 the centre was always the vertex mean, a stronger condition: the thin zone [[-70,-10],[70,0],[-70,10]] (all vertices x > 0, the vertex (70, 0) 112° from the mean) lost its refinement (tests/test_run.py `test_zone_in_a_hemisphere_not_of_its_vertex_mean`). All 45 zone polygons of docs/examples keep the mean, so their h is unchanged bit for bit | provisional | a zone larger than a hemisphere is needed |
| S20 | Resolution never comes from geometry (no pole clustering); every h comes from a layer | — | — | design rule | fixed | — |
| S21 | New criteria (bathymetry gradient, Rossby radius, model fields) are added as layers, without changes to the core | not built | — | design rule | fixed | — |
| S22 | Strait criterion h ≤ W / N_min, passages found without hand marking | built: N_min = 5 cells (`[coast] strait_n_min`, m14 `strait_n_min`); the passages of the coast's medial axis (S31, S32) get h = W/n within W/2 of the centreline, linear with `grad_max` to the base h, after the clip and before the limiter; run: `[coast] auto_straits` (default false, so existing configs do not change, as C26); m14: its strait blocks (B59) | lfs.py `auto_straits`, `PassageField`; run.py `size_field`; config.py `CoastCfg`; m14/strait.py | N_min of 5, 7 or 10 cells was considered, a parameter; 5 chosen, a listed strait's `cells` (C30) still overrides | provisional | the CORE2-like run with `auto_straits` (338 passages, 168 open, 143 zones, +426 nodes, the size stage +38 s; λ = h/2 there finds only passages wider than about h: 24 of the 89 C27 straits, not Gibraltar or the Bosphorus) |
| S23 | N_min counted in cells across | cells (fesom2_mesh_gen counts nodes: < 5 nodes = closed) | `strait_n_min` (S22) | design default | provisional | when the strait criterion is calibrated |
| S24 | Too-narrow passages are closed unless marked open; a marked-open passage gets h = W/N even below h_min | built for the automatic passages: a passage whose W/n falls below `[size] h_min_m` anywhere gets no zone (closed); marked open = a strait list entry (C26–C31), whose zone is not clipped | lfs.py `auto_straits`; run.py `size_field` | design default | provisional | — |
| S25 | Node-count estimate | ∫h⁻² dA over 2·10⁵ Fibonacci points of the whole sphere (land included) | run.py:96-98 | UI preview | provisional | a coast-aware estimate |
| S26 | Resolution ladder | 50 → 25 → 10 → 5 → 2 → 1 km; each rung measured (time, memory, quality) before the next; start near 50 km | — | design rule | fixed | — |
| S27 | Largest mesh | global uniform down to ~10 km (≈ 4·10⁶ nodes); finer rungs = h_min of a graded mesh with at most a few ×10⁷ nodes | — | a uniform 1 km ocean would be ≈ 4·10⁸ nodes | provisional | the scaling is measured |
| S28 | Mesh size on the Mac | ≤ ~4·10⁶ nodes (development, small tests) | — | 18 GB RAM of the development laptop | fixed | — |
| S29 | UI preview grid | lon-lat, `preview_deg` = 0.5° | config.py:128 | previews answer in seconds | provisional | — |
| S30 | Coastal refinement in a FESOM2-style default size function | none: h flat over the first ~20 rows from the coast; coastal or shelf refinement only as an optional broad layer, preferably depth-based (as in fesom2_mesh_gen's production meshes); when `coast_distance` is used its `plateau_m` should be ≥ ~20 h_m | config.md; docs/examples (`fesom2_core2like.toml` copies CORE2's exception: a 30 km plateau = 1 row) | coastal refinement is not mandatory, the default must not assume it; 6 of 7 reference FESOM2 meshes are flat within 20 rows (CORE2 0.81 at the coast is the exception; the one refined by depth gives 0.46 latitude-matched, not by distance) | fixed | a depth-based coastal layer is built |
| S31 | Strait (passage) rule and the strait block | a passage = a run of the medial axis (S32) with W ≤ W_max = 20 h and \|dW/ds\| ≤ n g (g = 0.1, dW/ds over ± W/2), n = max(N_min, round(median W/h)), both ends mouths: a junction, W > W_max beyond, a slope cut or an open-ocean leaf with wider water beyond (outward search of the axis to a width > 1.02 W_end, ≤ 20 000 vertices); a run ending in a dead end is no passage. Block (m14): only where h resolves the strait: \|median ln(W/(n h))\| ≤ 0.10 over the stations, else no block (h has no S22 zone there); ends = cross chords orthogonal to the centreline at the first and last station whose chord hits both bank loops within 1.5 r and is ≤ e^0.10 W, an end within h_b/2 of a coast corner (> 30°, C17) snapped onto it; h_b = W(s)/n (W smoothed along s at σ = 2 h_b); m = round ∫ds/h_b along the centreline, ≥ 2; no block when a side is shorter than half a cell or a vertex of another coast loop (an island) lies inside the outline; harmonic Dirichlet map on a conforming Delaunay mesh of the block polygon in its gnomonic plane at 0.33 h_b (unit cotangent weights, clipped ≥ 0), lattice at the integer points, 30 "size" sweeps (B25); acceptance B22 against h_b (every edge \|ln s/h_b\| ≤ 0.10, every quad angle ≤ 135°, none inverted), else split at the worst cross line (stations within W/2 dropped), depth ≤ 2, else left to the fill; an accepted block must also follow h (\|ln s/h\| p99 ≤ 0.10); largest first, a block within `block_gap` h of an accepted block of another passage dropped (the two pieces of a split are W apart and both stay) | lfs.py `passages`, `W_MAX`, `GRADING`, `OPEN_RATIO`, `OPEN_BUDGET`; m14/strait.py (`HIT_REACH`, `SNAP`, `FILTER`, `BG`, `MIN_M`, `SPLIT_DEPTH`, `TRIM`, `THETA_MAX`, `_Coast.islands_inside`); m14_blocks.py `strait_w_max`, `strait_grad`, `strait_n_min` | W_max: m14 note open question 2; \|dW/ds\| ≤ n g: h = W/n graded at g; m = round ∫ds/h taken with h = h_b (square cells where h ≠ W/n); the h tests: all resolution comes from h (S20), and without them the continent channel at scale 4 (150 km at h = 60 km) would get a 5-cell block of 30 km cells (ln 2 off h, tests/test_strait.py), and an accepted 20 km Alboran piece sat in the 13 km grading of the Tarifa zone (\|ln s/h\| p99 0.47); the e^0.10 chord test and MIN_M = 2 measured on Gibraltar (the Tarifa narrows: 13 km long, 14–19 km wide, m = 4, n = 5); unit weights: with h_b = W/n the flux W ξ' = const gives cells ∝ W | provisional | Gibraltar's narrows fail B22 (\|ln s/h_b\| 0.20 on the banks: 11.2 / 14.7 km against the 13.1 km centreline); a bend-aware size target or a relaxed acceptance for strait blocks |
| S32 | Medial-axis pruning | coast resampled every min(h/4, λ/2); ghost points ≥ 2 500 km from the coast (where the samples leave the sphere empty); (1) λ-medial axis, λ = 0.5 h (run: 0.5 min(h, N_min h_min), so every passage S24 keeps open survives); (2) potential residue: two of a vertex's samples on different loops or ≥ 2 W of coast between them beyond their chord; (3) dead-end branches (never wider than 1.02 × W at their junction, not open-ocean leaves) removed, repeated until none is left | lfs.py `medial_axis`, `LAM`, `STEP`, `GHOST_M`, `RESIDUE`, `OPEN_RATIO`, `DEAD_ROUNDS` | Chazal & Lieutier (λ), Ogniewicz & Kübler (residue); measured on GSHHS i at h = min(25 km, the S22 zone, h_min 2 km) around Gibraltar: λ alone leaves 145 of the 258 vertices within 40 km of Tarifa as junctions and cuts the narrows into 13 pieces of 0.8–8 km; with (2) 69 vertices, none a junction, one passage 13.8–18.5 km wide and 19 km long; (3) changes nothing there | provisional | a coast filter (C16) built before the axis |

| S34 | Layer order and zone modes | layers applied in file order to a running h (the first layer starts it; a `set` / `smooth` zone placed first acts on the base h): every kind but `zone`, and zone `mode = "min"` (default): h = min(h, layer); `"set"`: h_m inside the polygon whatever the running h, linear in the geodesic distance from h_m to the running h over `transition_m` (0: a step), `h_out_m` unused; `"smooth"`: (1 − w) h + w s, s = the smoothed running h (S36), w = 1 inside, linear to 0 at `transition_m`; later layers apply on top, a min layer only where it is below the base h (neutral at the base, so a later zone does not undo a set zone's raise above the base outside its own reach); clip, strait zones and limiter after all layers. Only min layers: the old `Minimum` (bit-identical h). The limiter grid's floor (`run.h_floor`) skips smooth zones and the `h_out_m` of set zones | `meshom/size.py` `layered`, `Layered`, `SetZone`, `SmoothZone`; `meshom/run.py` `layer_fields`, `background_h`, `h_floor`; `config.ZoneLayer.mode` | the user sets and smooths h in a region; min commutes, so old configs are unchanged: checked bit for bit on 4 example configs and mesh A2 (background h and limited h at 1.5 M points, limiter nodes) | provisional | a mode on other kinds (e.g. a coast layer that sets) is needed |
| S35 | h variants (`[size] from`) | `from` = a file of `[size]` and `[[size.layers]]` only (else an error), relative to the config's folder (`load`; `loads` / `from_dict`: `base_dir`, default the working directory); its keys and layers first, the config's `[size]` keys override, its layers are appended; a variant's own `from` is followed; `load` inlines it: `dumps` and the run's `config.resolved.toml` carry the merged layers and no `from` (`Config.size_from` keeps the resolved path, not written) | `meshom/config.py` `_size_tables`, `loads_size`, `dumps_size`, `Config.size_from` | saved h variants; a run stays reproducible when the variant file changes | provisional | the UI needs `from` kept on save |
| S36 | Smooth-zone numerics | s = exp of the mean of ln h over the geodesic disc of radius `smooth_m`, weights (1 − (r/smooth_m)²)² (biweight) × node area; s held on an equiangular cubed sphere at smooth_m / `SMOOTH_CELLS` = 12 (the nodes within transition_m + 2 cells of the polygon; bilinear between them); ln h sampled on a second cubed sphere at half the smallest layer h (as the limiter), capped at `LIMITER_MAX_NODES` and coarsened to ≤ `SMOOTH_MAX_K` = 20 000 nodes per disc; neighbours by KD-tree k-nearest (k = the disc's most nodes at cells 0.70 × nominal, + 10 %) | `meshom/size.py` `smoothed`, `SMOOTH_CELLS`, `SMOOTH_MAX_K`, `_cube_area_density` | measured on a 4° 20 km box in 100 km, smooth_m 300 km, against a fine polar quadrature (21 482 m at the box centre): s held at smooth_m/6 and sampled there +5.4 %; sampled at 10 km +2.3 % (of it ~1.4 % the bilinear error of the 50 km grid); held at smooth_m/12: +1.4 % (10 km samples), +0.6 % (5.6 km); the biweight has no jump when a node enters the disc (a top hat would) | provisional | smoothing of very large zones is too slow for the UI preview |
| S37 | Zone mode `blend`: h between two edges (`meshom.size.BlendZone`) | h_m inside `polygon`, the running h outside `outer`, between them ln h = (1 − t) ln h_m + t ln h_e, t = d_in / (d_in + d_out) (geodesic distances to the two edges), h_e = the running h at the nearest sample of the outer edge (bound once by `layered`), ln h_e averaged over ±`BLEND_EDGE_SMOOTH_M` = 50 km along the edge; the edges sampled every `BLEND_SAMPLE_M` = 1 km (distance error ≤ 0.5 km); both polygons great-circle, in an open hemisphere, the polygon inside the outer one; `transition_m` not used | size.py `BlendZone`, `blend_zone`, `layered`; config.py `ZoneLayer.outer`; run.py `layer_fields` | the ORCA025 + Arctic mesh: the rise from 4.5 km to ORCA's cells over whatever lies between ORCA's edge and the 4.5 km edge (a few hundred km at Hudson Bay, ~1500-2000 km in the Atlantic), gentle where they are far apart (a narrow Atlantic ramp, as in the FArc mesh, is not wanted); a `set` zone has one transition width. h_e, not the running h at the point: the running h of an ORCA layer collapses near NEMO's grid poles (73 E 50 N, 107 W 66 N), which lie in the blend; the 50 km average removes ORCA's cell-to-cell steps and keeps h_e continuous along the edge (it jumps only across the outer polygon's medial axis, where the two nearest edge parts differ; the limiter grades it) | exploration | when a mesh shows a seam of h across a medial line of the outer edge |

| C51 | Narrow through-passages: at least K cells across | `[coast] passage_min_cells` = K (default 2; 3 for meshes where straits matter; 0 or 1 = off): every through-passage of the final mesh, listed or not, gets at least K cells across at its narrowest section. Measure (topological): ring distance from the coast nodes (nodes sharing an element one ring apart); quads split along the diagonal with fewer core corners; core nodes ≥ K // 2 rings; triangles core-linked across an edge with ≥ 1 core end (K even) or 2 (K odd); pools = core-link components holding a core node (K even) or an all-core triangle (K odd); necks = the rest joined by their links, or one link between two pool triangles. Through-passage: a neck reaching two pools, or one pool from ≥ 2 ends with its coast on ≥ 2 boundary loops; left alone: one end (a one-cell fjord, a staircase corner) and a dead-end inlet = the only neck to pools below `BASIN_MIN_H2` = 8 h² (h = the neck's mean edge; the side with the largest pool never). Fix: a `widen` corridor (C45 formula, `cells` = K, h_m = the background h at its largest on the path) along the path pool → rim triangles (fewest) → pool, `SMOOTH_PASSES` = 2 three-point smoothings, extended `EXT_H` = 1 h into both pools; `cut`: cut again from the uncut mesh, ≤ `MAX_ROUNDS` = 3 rounds; `loops`: the run again with a `[[straits]]` entry per passage, ≤ 3 rebuilds; a passage found again inside an earlier corridor gets one cell more. BASIN_MIN_H2: the bridged basins measured 1.4–7.7 h² (one to three interior nodes in a staircase recess) apart from 9.5–632 h² (Puget Sound 12.4, the Ob Gulf 30–53, the Red Sea 490, the Baltic 632) on A2 and the old twin. Measured: mesh A2 (m14, cut, 90 widened straits) 49 → 0 in 2 rounds (51 corridors 113–150 km wide), land cut away 289 458 km² (+35 % on the 827 556 km² of the straits' corridors, +0.09 % water), 24 islands removed (37 024 km², largest 7 890 km²), 144 439 → 144 691 nodes, 39/39 → 39/39; the m15_tri twin 29 → 0 in 1 round, 87 673 km² (+27 % on 324 194), 3 islands (1 095 km²), 163 263 → 163 386 nodes, 29/29 → 29/29; glob100 loops 0 at K = 2 (39/39); "modest" = below 0.1 % of the water and about a third of the listed straits' own cut, so on by default | provisional | loops at K = 3 does not converge: each rebuild re-meshes globally and new 2-cell passages appear elsewhere (glob100: 5 → 2 → 1 → 2 after 3 rebuilds, 39/39, 36 886 → 37 382 nodes); closing instead of widening (a thin barrier) not needed so far; an economical first corridor (the triangle margin on quads, then escalate) |
| C52 | The water check of every written mesh | meshom's runs: `write_files` (the run's write stage, `meshom write`) records the mesh's edge-connected water components for every format (`water_record`: C44's `water_components`, `connectivity_ok` = one component); more than one adds `water_components` to the fesom2 / fesom3 checks' `stop`, and `meshom run` / `meshom write` print a `WATER NOT CONNECTED` line naming each extra component (elements, nodes, km2, box); the run goes on (C44). The FESOM3 ORCA path: `fesom3.write` refuses (ValueError, before any file) a mesh of more than one component (`water_check`; with cavities joined only at a common layer of the vertex columns, B170), naming each extra one (elements, nodes, centre, box, layer counts). 2-D components (and the cavity mesh's joined at a common layer), not per depth level | run.py `write_files`, `water_record`, `water_text`, `write_line`, `run`; cli.py `_run`; orca/fesom3.py `water_check`, `write`; tests `tests/test_straits.py::test_every_written_mesh_gets_the_water_check`, `tests/test_run.py::test_run_again_after_the_partitioner`, `tests/test_orca_fesom3.py::test_closed_water_removed` | every mesh meshom writes is checked; before, the water was checked only in the straits stage (C44, runs with straits) and not at all on the ORCA path, where a cavity-free eORCA36 kept 13 closed pockets (B170); FESOM's initial-state fill sweeps until every surface node has a value, so a cut-off piece without climatology never ends it; per depth level the water splits normally (basins below their sills) and FESOM fills those levels from above, so per-level counts are no error | provisional | a regional mode (open boundaries; several pieces may be intended); `keep_basins_km2` runs (C48) and m16_orca's `keep_seas = true` (B84, B85, B171: NEMO's closed seas kept on purpose) are reported as failing by design |

| B78 | NEMO's ORCA generator in meshom (`meshom.orca.generator`) | G. Madec's tripolar generator with A. Sellar's tropical stretch (NEMO, CeCILL licence; the copy at github.com/NCAR/tx1_4; see NOTICE) ported to NumPy / Numba, each law citing its Fortran routine in orca/generator.py: `set_coefficients` (FSA = FSPHIY(FSY0P(FSFA + jpeq)) with FSFA's atan saturation and cubic term ra1 = 1e-5, ra2 by the code's bisection on [0.1, 10] so that the ellipses end on the segment between the poles; FSB = FSPHIY(FSFB FSY0P), FSFB = 1 + rb1 tanh(rb2 x^3), rb2 = 1, rb1 from FSB(pole line) = 0; FSY0 with ry0 from the poles' middle), `hgr_ctl` (the boundary values to 1e-14, A and B decreasing, \|dY0\| <= \|dA\|, by central differences 1e-4 instead of the code's analytic derivatives, whose FSDFA does not match FSFA), `nth_car` (each meridian of the north half-grid integrated in x over jph = jpint jpfac (2 rjpnth - 2) + 1 points, jpfac 5, jpint 10, Matsuno start, leapfrog, Asselin filter 0.1, the ellipse index of each point by ncompt bisections (the first n with 0.5^n (jpjn - 1) < 1e-14), the points placed on ellipse j where its normal has the meridian's slope interpolated linearly in j; the pole line's points clamped between the poles; master meridians by the sign of y), `msh_sth` (Mercator rows asin(tanh(rdx (j - jpeqt)))), `msh_nth` (the mirror about the second pole's U-F column, the fold row, the two wrap columns, the rotation by rlampo), `msh_trp` / `trop` / `shoots` (dphi/dj = R cos phi - (R - Rmin)(e^-(c phi)^n/a^n + e^-(c phi)^n/b^n)/2, the join at the unstretched T row nearest pphi_join, c by the code's Newton (1e-6 increment and tolerances), the j(phi) table of the last integration kept as the code does). Two extensions: the pivot (`pivot` "T": rjpnth = jpnord, the older version's T-U fold the user guide 5.2.4 names for ORCA2) and the lattice (`grid(..., q, i_offset, j_offset)`: the map of the continuous indices evaluated on the 1/q lattice, the user guide's "quadrature"; rows beyond the pole line by the fold's symmetry P(I, J) = P(2 - I, 2 J_p - J)). The code's own layout is the q = 1 lattice with T at (i + 1/2, j + 1) to 1e-13 deg. Scale factors e1, e2 are not computed (a mesh needs only the points) | orca/generator.py (`set_coefficients`, `hgr_ctl`, `_meridian`, `_meridians`, `north_points`, `trop_rows`, `row_map`, `lattice`, `_grid_q1`, `_grid_lattice`, `grid`); tests `tests/test_orca.py::test_set_coefficients_orca1`, `::test_code_layout_is_the_lattice`, `::test_orca1_is_eorca1` | the grids can be repeated only from their code. | B114 | The ladder's edges and rows (`m16_orca` `transition = "ladder"`, `ladder_lat`; `meshom.orca.ladder.edge_rows`, `rows`) | between a coarse ORCA part and its `fine_orca` refinement (k = 3) two plain rows replace the staircases: the coarse part's T rows up to R_c = its last F row lying wholly south of `ladder_lat[0]`, the fine part's T rows from R_f + 1 to the fold in every column (R_f = its last F row lying wholly south of `ladder_lat[1]`: it covers everything north of it); between them, in fine F indices (x, y), the strip's rows are j-lines y_k from y0 = 3 R_c + b_j to y1 = R_f, the row height h(d) = 3 x 3^(-d / H) (H = y1 - y0, d = y - y0: ln h linear in d as B110's blend), n = round(2 H / (3 ln 3)) rows at equal steps of N(d) = H (3^(d/H) - 1) / (3 ln 3). The map is evaluated at fractional (x, y) by `generator.lattice` every 1/SUB = 1/8 fine column and interpolated linearly along the row; the edge rows are the grids' own F points. | B195 | Harmonic coordinates of a coastal strip (`meshom/insert/harmonic.py` `harmonic_map`, `HarmonicMap`, `_split_chords`, `MapError`, `HB_CELLS`, `MAX_POINTS`, `ARC_SHARE`; `insert/realcoast.py` `NEST_PASSES`; `[[inserts]] rows`) | `rows = "harmonic"` (default for strips): the band region (the spine P -> Q, the bank beyond Q, the host chain, the bank before P) is triangulated (boundary and interior points every HB_CELLS 0.4 fine cells, a triangular lattice, constrained Delaunay; the spacing grows by 1.6 while the count would pass MAX_POINTS 150000; every interior edge with both ends on one side of the square (the corners P, Q, B0, A0 on two sides) is split at its midpoint, the two triangles at it split in two each, until none is left); t solves Laplace (P1, cotangent weights) with t = 0 on the spine, 1 on the chain, no flux through the banks; s with s = 0 / 1 on the banks, no flux through the spine and the chain (the conformal map onto a rectangle); the boundary values (s on the spine and chain, t on the banks) are blended with ARC_SHARE 0.1 of the normalised arc length, made strictly increasing per side, and the interior is solved again with mean value weights onto the unit square: a one-to-one piecewise linear map whose inverse X(s, t) places every node. Every triangle of the map must have a strictly positive area of one orientation in the square; a map with a flipped or flat triangle is refused (the build's record says why), never masked. A strip's coast (bridge = "narrow") repeats the pocket search on the bridged coast NEST_PASSES 3 times, bridging there only mouths below 3 cells (AUTO_MOUTH_H): the outer channel of an estuary whose narrows were bridged first. Credit: conformal grids of coastal models after P. Sakov's gridgen (github.com/sakov/gridgen-c); Tutte 1963 (doi:10.1112/plms/s3-13.1.743); Floater 2003 (doi:10.1016/S0167-8396(03)00002-5; one-to-one condition: doi:10.1090/S0025-5718-02-01466-7) | insert/harmonic.py, insert/band.py, insert/realcoast.py | positive weights on a convex image give a one-to-one discrete map (Tutte, Floater), so a lattice pulled back through it cannot fold; the mean value weights are positive on any triangulation, cotangent weights are not. On a square (sides of collinear points) this holds only when no interior edge joins two points of one side: such an edge maps into that side, and a boundary ear (three consecutive points of one side, no interior point) has zero area. Adding the centroid of a triangle with three boundary corners did not remove it (the new point's neighbours were the ear's corners and it took the side's value): Brittany 3 zero-area triangles (0.53 km²), a synthetic notch 4 km wide and 6 km deep (0.8 km spacing) 3, accepted as "ok" with those triangles masked; splitting the edges leaves none. Without ARC_SHARE the Tagus mouth's outer channel (about 4 km wide, 10 km long, left after its narrows were bridged) squeezed the spine's s there to steps of 1e-6 and the map's triangles to zero area; with one pocket pass that channel stays in the coast (synthetic estuary: the coast 3.7 km into the channel; 3 passes: 0.6 km); bridging every non-meshed pocket in the later passes bridged two shallow bays with mouths of 54 and 59 km in ORCA025 (coast shift 20.8 km; with the 3-cell limit 6.6 km) | provisional | corridors (two host chains) on the same map |
| B196 | Rows of the harmonic band (`harmonic.rows_t`, `ROW_SMOOTH`, `T_SAMPLES`, `S_PER_CELL`) | rows are graphs t = T_k(s) increasing in k at every s (a column s = const crosses each row once); row k lies at the arc length d_k (B181's distances: h_fine over the band, then + 0.12 per unit distance up to the host size) along each column, its log t smoothed along the row with a Gaussian of ROW_SMOOTH 2 x d_k, and blends (smoothstep of (d_k - band) / (d_{K-1} - band)) into the level line t = tau_k (tau_k: the level at d_k on the middle 80 % of the columns), the last row before the chain on it; where two rows come closer than a quarter of their mean step in t, the outer one is pushed out (never crossing). Columns sampled at S_PER_CELL 4 per spine cell, each in T_SAMPLES 400 steps of t stretched toward the coast | insert/harmonic.py | the lattice cells tile the square, so the band's cells tile the region; only a cell's straight edges can fold where the map bends within one cell. Rows at the distance alone are not orthogonal to curved columns (round a cape the columns from its flanks curve, the distance rows tilt): Portugal before the cleaning 304 of 2187 core quads > 120 deg; ROW_SMOOTH 0.5 / 1 / 2 / 3: 140 / 61 / 35 / 39 (smallest cell 2.05 / 2.00 / 1.67 / 1.46 km). Level lines alone are squeezed at capes and spread in bays; near the chain they follow its steps for a cell and smooth them out (the outer rows do not repeat a zigzag chain as rows at the distance would) | provisional | — |
| B197 | Columns, corners and the polish of the harmonic band (`harmonic.build_harmonic`, `_events`, `_relax`, `_drift`, `SPLIT`, `THETA_DEG`, `TILT_DEG`, `EQUALIZE`, `DRIFT_ROWS`, `DRIFT_COLS`, `RELAX_BETA`, `ALIGN_ROWS`, `_align`, `reflex_corners`, `REFLEX_DEG`, `_collapse`, `polish`, `POLISH_ROUNDS`, `POLISH_STEPS`, `POLISH_DEG`, `POLISH_FREE_DEG`, `POLISH_TRI_DEG`, `POLISH_EDGE`) | row 0's columns are the spine nodes; each next row keeps its inner neighbour's columns (s) and splits a column when the mean of its two cells along the row exceeds SPLIT 1.22 x the row's size (its two arms THETA_DEG 18 deg apart over the row step), merges two when the mean of three cells is below h / SPLIT (the smaller middle one), at most max(1, 0.2 N) events per row, none at a column or next to one that changed within 2 rows (block.GAP_ROWS); then the row's columns by least squares near their straight continuation with smooth widths (RELAX_BETA 1 on the second differences, the ends fixed, kept in order); in the last ALIGN_ROWS 4 rows the columns are matched to the chain's nodes (monotone dynamic programming, cost (gap / chain spacing)^2, 1 per unmatched), the unmatched merged or split, the matched drawn onto their node by 1 / (rows left); a chain node that turns away from the band by >= REFLEX_DEG 60 deg (chords over 2 edges) takes two columns (a triangle), one that turns toward it none (B180's corner triangle); neighbouring rows zipped by s (block.zip_rows); P and Q merged into row 1's ends on the banks (the corner cell a triangle). After B186's smoothing and repair, the polish: the free nodes of quads (rows off the chain's ring) with a corner > POLISH_DEG 120 deg and one ring round them move to the trial position (0.5, 0.25, 0.1, 0.04 x the shortest edge in 8 directions, or the neighbours' mean) that lowers the sum of squared excesses of the cells' largest corners over 100 deg (triangles: 140 deg), cells convex, no edge below POLISH_EDGE 0.8 x its ends' shortest edge before the polish; at most 20 rounds | insert/harmonic.py, insert/band.py | a split or a merge is a valence-5 Y node and one triangle (the ladder of B114-B116); a column that drifts toward equal spacing on every row slants the cells (Portugal: 291 core quads > 120 before smoothing; drift only within DRIFT_COLS 2 columns of an event of the last DRIFT_ROWS 4 rows when RELAX_BETA is 0); P and Q lie where the coast runs straight, the square's corner there is a cell with a 180 deg corner; without the reflex rule an ORCA staircase step left a 146 deg cell. ## 5. Cross field and cones

| # | rule | value now | where | why | status | revisit when |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| F1 | The organising identity | ρ_c = (2/π)(K + Δ ln h) is the net density of irregular spots; Σ(4 − deg) + T = 4χ (= 8 on the sphere) | compete.py:3-4, 89-139 | discrete Gauss-Bonnet in the conformal metric g/h² (F2) | fixed | — |
| F2 | Connection of the field | Levi-Civita of g plus ⋆d ln h (the conformal metric g/h²) | field.py:1-25 | in the metric g/h² the target cells have unit size, so the field is built there | fixed | — |
| F3 | Default field | `"cones"`: a trivial-connection field with cones placed from ρ_c (Crane 2010); `"smoothest"` (Knöppel) as an option | field.py:29-48, 534-535; m02_linf_frontal.py:39 | measured: the smoothest field twists instead of making the ρ_c cones (field.py:49-55) | provisional | m07 found that position-field methods do better with the 8 cube cones |
| F4 | Field symmetry | n = 4 for quads, n = 6 (asterisk) for triangles | field.py:534; m02_linf_frontal.py:38 | Georgiadis 2017 | fixed | — |
| F5 | Smoothing of the curvature charge before quantising | heat kernel of 0.5 background cells | `CONE_SMOOTH_CELLS`, field.py:77 | cancels triangle-scale noise of the discrete Δ ln h; arbitrary, first guess | provisional | — |
| F6 | No cone near a fixed coast | charge within 3 h of the coast moves to the 3 h shell; in a strait narrower than 6 h the cones go to its mouths | `COAST_CONE_DISTANCE`, field.py:78, 32-35 | keeps the coastal rows free of cones | provisional | coastal layers (M5) |
| F7 | Quantisation of the charge | round(Σq⁺) cones of +1/n, the rest of round(Σq) as −1/n, by a mass-balanced k-d partition; each charge part on its own | field.py:35-40, 350-387 | charges of different spatial scale must not share leaves | provisional | — |
| F8 | Cones on or off | `cones` = True; False only for m14 block frames (no cone, free boundary) | field.py:534-542 | a block's map needs a field without cones on its region (m14 note) | fixed | — |
| F9 | MBO smoothing of the field | 0 steps | `smooth_steps`, m02_linf_frontal.py:40; field.py:57-61 | MBO pulls ± pairs together (disc rim +17 → +10) | provisional | — |
| F10 | Field at coast nodes | fixed to the boundary tangent | field.py:57-58 | coast-aligned rows | fixed | — |
| F11 | Quad orientation in the open ocean | free (not pinned to rotated lat-lon) | — | pinning costs ≈ π/(2g) ≈ 16× more column ends on a test disc | fixed | — |
| F12 | Transition element | isolated triangles by default; 3/5-valent pairs as an option | — | which one FESOM tolerates better is unmeasured | open | a FESOM run compares the two |
| F13 | ρ_c integration | a background mesh of ≈ 1.5·10⁵ nodes; the coast's geodesic curvature is not included | compete.py:89-116 | ρ_c does not depend on the scale; 2–3 s per call | provisional | coastal ρ_c matters |
| F14 | Singular points placed on land when h allows | not built | — | every global ocean grid puts its poles and cube corners on land | open | whether meshom should do the same |
| F15 | Several water components (a lake, a closed sea kept as its own loop) | one BFS tree per connected component of the edge graph, rooted and anchored at its first fixed node (else the first corner of its first triangle); the cones quantised per triangle component with its own total (its integer charge, the F7 rule unchanged inside), its coastal charge pushed to its own 3 h shell (F6); the Neumann mean removed per component | field.py:39-48 (docstring), 449-487, 494-507, 533-560 (`_trivial_connection_field`, `_BFSTree`) | the component without the single root kept ψ = 0 (seam_coast's lake 31.4° off its coast); the global k-d partition put a −¼ cone into seam_strait's lake (charge 0) and a small lake's +1 into the ocean's shell, so the trivial connection had no solution (CG residual 1.5·10³, 5.5·10⁶) and the field broke | fixed | — |

## 6. Shared defaults of the methods and the measures

| # | rule | value now | where | why | status | revisit when |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| M1 | Common smoothing of the runner | kind "angle", 10 iterations, boundary nodes fixed | compete.py:181-188; smooth.py:235-236 | the same post-processing for every method | provisional | coast nodes may slide (C21) |
| M2 | Relaxation of the smoothing | 1.0 for "angle" and "size", 0.5 for "laplace" | smooth.py:239-250 | 0.5 damps Laplace's checkerboard mode | provisional | — |
| M3 | Smart guard of the smoothing | a move is undone when the worst element around the node gets worse and is > 45° from ideal (triangle angles 15–105° and quad angles 45–135° are free); and, independently, when an element around the node that had every corner below 180° gets one at or above it (inverted, or a non-convex quad: badness ≥ 180° − ideal) | `tol_deg`, smooth.py:24-31 `_revert` | ≤ 30° freezes r-adaptation within ~10 sweeps (59 % of Poisson-Delaunay nodes touch a triangle beyond 30°). Before 2026-10-27 only the star's worst element was compared, so a move that improved a poor triangle (worst 106.9° → 105.4° off ideal) could make a convex quad of the same star reflex (185.3° corner, scaled Jacobian +0.082 → −0.092), and it was written to FESOM-C files (`tests/test_smooth.py::test_smart_guard_keeps_quads_convex` fails without the second rule). A mesh whose moves never break an element is unchanged bit for bit: fesom2_core2like (m15_tri, 162 398 nodes, 29/29 gates) and global_150km_m14 run as m14_blocks_rep (59 341 nodes, 39/39 gates) give identical raw / optimized npz and FESOM files without and with the second rule | provisional | — |
| M4 | Global scale of kind "size" | λ = √(Σ area / Σ ideal area at h), once per call | smooth.py:71-81 | the node count fixes the scale | provisional | — |
| M5 | Recombination cost | max_k \|θ_k − 90°\| / 90° in the quad's tangent plane; ∞ for a non-convex quad | recombine.py:8-10, 40-46 | the worst corner decides | provisional | — |
| M6 | Price of a leftover triangle | 1/3 (m01, m02, m07, m11): a pair is merged only when all corners of the quad lie within 30°–150° | m02_linf_frontal.py:47; m01_isotropic.py:40; m07_instant_meshes.py:33; m11_local_ops.py:25 | gain 2p − c > 0 ⇔ c < 2/3 ⇔ \|θ − 90°\| < 60°: matches the 150° hard gate | provisional | a price field p(x), 0 in triangle zones (not built) |
| M7 | Matching | greedy by gain, then augmenting paths of ≤ 10 matched pairs; exact (networkx) only on small meshes | recombine.py:12-14, 25 | greedy leaves ≤ 1.2× the triangles of exact | provisional | — |
| M8 | m02 L∞ exclusion box | half-size 0.75 × the mean h of the two points (Gmsh uses 0.7) | `alpha`, m02_linf_frontal.py:41 | Remacle 2013, Gmsh algorithm 8 | provisional | — |
| M9 | m02 seam points | a step blocked at a gap ≥ 1.414 h gets a point at the middle of the gap, exclusion 0.6 | `seam`, `alpha_seam`, m02_linf_frontal.py:43-44 | Remacle 2013 §5 (off-centre); arbitrary, first guess | provisional | — |
| M10 | m02 post-processing | 30 "size" sweeps + 3 "angle" sweeps, coast fixed | m02_linf_frontal.py:49-50 | — | provisional | — |
| M11 | Background mesh spacing | 0.5 h by default; m02 and m14 use 1.0 h | delaunay.py:485; m02_linf_frontal.py:36 | 0.5 h is 3–4× slower and no better at scale 1 (m02) | provisional | — |
| M12 | Poisson-disc exclusion | α = 0.85 (nearest-neighbour p1 / p50 / p99 = 0.85 / 0.90 / 1.15 h) | delaunay.py:417-429 | α ≈ 0.75 centres the Delaunay edges on h | provisional | — |
| M13 | Orientation and in-circle predicates | fp64 with a relative tie tolerance 1e-12; ties never flip; not exact arithmetic | delaunay.py:7-11, 26 | filtered exact predicates (Shewchuk) were planned and are not built | provisional | a robustness failure on regular point sets or at 10⁷ nodes |
| M14 | Size measure per edge | s = great-circle length; h at the normalised chord midpoint | metrics.py:164-176 | mutants "h at an end node" and "size in lon-lat" are killed by tests | fixed | — |
| M15 | Angles | in each element's gnomonic tangent plane, never in lon-lat; quad SJ = the smallest corner sine (< 0 at a reflex corner) | quality/kernel.py:1-5 | lon-lat angles are distorted; ≤ 0.22° from true spherical angles at scale 4 | fixed | — |
| M16 | Element size ratio | mean edge length of the two elements across each interior edge (what centroid velocities see) | metrics.py:125-134 | FESOM's velocities sit at element centroids, so they see the jump between neighbours | fixed | — |
| M17 | Geometry and data | points are fp64 unit vectors (N,3); elements CCW seen from outside; int64 indices | geo.py:3 | no pole or antimeridian case; fp64 on A100 | fixed | — |
| M18 | Earth radius | R = 6 371 000 m | geo.py:7 | the sphere of JIGSAW and of fesom2_mesh_gen (D. Sein, AWI); FESOM-C uses 6.4e6 m, FESOM2 6 367 500 m | provisional | FESOM3's r_earth is known (lengths change by ≤ 0.5 %) |
| M19 | Method timeout | 1800 s per run (`meshom compete`, `meshom run`); 7200 s in the scaling run | compete.py:233; cli.py:137; config.py:134 | arbitrary, first guess | provisional | runs at 10⁶–10⁷ nodes |
| M20 | Guards inside methods that reuse gate values | m11 keeps angles ≤ 150° and edges within [0.7, 1.4] h; m07's extraction badness is normalised by 150° / 30° (quads) and 120° / 20° (triangles) | m11_local_ops.py:25; m11/ops.py:405-406; m07/extract.py:150-154 | the same limits for every case | provisional | the gates change |
| M21 | Reproducibility | the same input on the same hardware gives the same mesh where cheap; no bitwise requirement; measured bit-identical at 4 and 16 threads, not across platforms | — | — | fixed | — |
| M22 | Numerical stack | Numba for CPU loops and topology, JAX for device geometry and gradients, fp64 everywhere | `environment.yml` | a CPU framework benchmark | provisional | a GPU benchmark |
| M23 | Population-control thresholds (m15_tri, the m14 tri fill) | delete one end of each edge with ln(s/h) < −0.18; insert the midpoint of each edge with ln(s/h) > +0.22 | `T_DEL`, `T_INS`, popctl.py:34-35 | −0.14 / +0.18 create defects (p99 0.195 / 0.199 against 0.114–0.129) | provisional | ramp-heavy h (real coasts, g = 0.1 everywhere) |
