| M34 | Valence flips | flip edge (a, b) to (c, d) when Σ(deg − 6)² over the interior nodes falls, the quad is strictly convex, the new edge has \|ln(len / h)\| ≤ 0.35 and a, b keep > 4 edges; on in the m14 tri fill, off in m15_tri | `lh_tol`, popctl.py:137; `tri_vflips`, m14_blocks.py:80; `vflips`, m15_tri.py:36 | m14_blocks_tri continent 20/23 → 22/23, uniform / disc / band unchanged; m15_tri irregular 1.06 → 1.03 %; 0.35 arbitrary, first guess | provisional | — |
| M35 | m15_tri pipeline | seeded packing → sphere Delaunay with the coast loops recovered (conforming) → population control → size fit; no CVT stage (S5 of the m15 note) | m15_tri.py | S4 met every acceptance number without S5 (m15 note) | provisional | real coasts; the cut coast (C1) |
| M36 | Far-cap helper points of the sphere Delaunay | when a hull facet's plane passes within 1e-9 of the origin or beyond it (every point within one hemisphere: a lake alone, a regional domain), or when Qhull finds no 3-d hull (fewer than 4 points or all on one circle: a 3-node lake, a 4-node co-circular cavity, popctl deleting the only interior node of one), 7 helpers close the hull before the edge recovery and the land removal: the antipode of the points' mean direction m and a ring of 6 at ρ = (π − r_max)/2 from it (r_max = the largest angle from m to a point, so the ring lies ρ from the antipode and ρ beyond the points' cap); their triangles are dropped at the end; points around the origin get no helper | `HULL_MARGIN`, `HELPER_RING`, `far_cap_helpers`, `sphere_delaunay(points, edges, coast)`, delaunay.py:27-28, 309-348; every caller that recovered a coast in three steps now passes its edges (background_mesh, popctl, m01, m02, m03, m04, m07, m09, m11, m14 stitch_mix); the lakes alone: `ring=False` of the cases_seams builders | lakes alone: background mesh χ −15 / −13, m02 / m14 Euler −28 / −26, and the edge recovery hung on exactly great-circle coasts (an earlier probe closed the hull with one antipodal dummy); the no-hull case raised Qhull's QH6214 / QH6013 before the helpers were tried (tests/test_delaunay.py `test_small_contour_alone`: 1 and 2 triangles now; a hull that exists takes the old path unchanged); the margin only catches a hull whose origin is on or outside it, so the competition cases (background points at scale 4: the origin 0.967–0.9996 inside every facet) are bit-identical (33 methods and 2 background meshes × 4 cases at scale 4; 8 methods and 1 background mesh × uniform, continent at scale 1); ring size arbitrary, first guess | provisional | a regional domain near a hemisphere wide (r_max → π) |
| M37 | m02 point buffer | starts at 2 n_est + seeds + 1000 and doubles at most PACK_DOUBLINGS = 3 times (16 n_est), then `points` raises RuntimeError (with whether the field is finite) | m02_linf_frontal.py `PACK_DOUBLINGS`, `points` | a NaN field packs NaN points without end (a NaN candidate is never excluded), so the doubling never stopped (the conforming-m14 hang); old source: the NaN-field packing did not end in 120 s, now the error in < 10 s (tests/test_coast_uncross.py); the bound is arbitrary, a first guess | provisional | a legitimate packing needs more than 16 n_est |
| M38 | Smoothness within the size tolerance (`size_fit.smooth_fit`) | free nodes minimise mean over interior edges of ln(M_a/M_b)² (M = the mean edge chord of the two elements: G7's ratio; p = 2) + w_tol 10³ · mean Huber(\|ln s/h\| − (t − 0.01), quadratic up to 0.05 then linear) + mean over corners [0.1 (θ − θ*)² + 100 (θ outside 60–120° quad / 35–100° triangle)² + 10⁴ (θ outside 35–145° / 25–115°)²] + size_fit's barrier; θ = the corner angle about the outward normal (atan2, not its cosine); L-BFGS 500 iterations × ≤ 5 rounds (h frozen per round), size_fit's chart and inversion guard. Acts as the last stage of m14's stitch (every fill and `_mix`; block and coast nodes fixed; the `tri` fill skips its size fit) and of m15_tri (instead of the size fit, Lawson flips as `fit_flips`) when `size_tolerance` is set (m14: 0.20 by default, G38; 0 = off) | size_fit.py `smooth_fit`, `smooth_problem`, `QUAD_LO` … `TRI_HI2`; m14/stitch.py `tolerance_fit`; m14/stitch_mix.py; m15_tri.py; m14_blocks.py and m15_tri.py `tol_fit` (options); `m14_blocks_rep`: after the repair (R22) | measured on the CORE2-like m14 fill (closed sphere, cut afterwards, before the repair; docs/methods/m14_blocks.md): p = 4 left the ratio at 1.175 (p = 2: 1.11); w_angle 1 left it at 1.16; a cosine wall let quads flatten to 180°; w_wall 10 left a 151.6° quad, 100 none; a hard angle guard in the line search stalled at the first corner it reached; the stiff wall at the gates themselves let a quad reach 150.1°, 5° inside none; without the 0.01 margin the soft wall settled 0.004–0.007 beyond t; 3 rounds left 0.10 at p99 0.103, 5 rounds 0.098; w_tol 10⁵ folded the test grid | provisional | FESOM3 runs on smoothed meshes; a mesh where the tolerance cannot be met (the Huber pull) |
| M39 | Edge recovery at a node outside the triangulation | `recover_edges` raises RuntimeError when a constraint ends at a node in no triangle (a point the hull left out: a duplicate of another point), naming the constraint, the node, the node it coincides with and the distance; every fan walk of `_find_edge` and of `_recover` is bounded by the triangle count and stops at a node in no triangle | delaunay.py `recover_edges`, `_find_edge`, `_recover` | Qhull keeps one of two coincident points; the other has no vertex triangle (vt = −1), the fan walk started at triangle −1 (the last one), never met its start and spun forever: the B63 hang (> 40 min twice; `max_iter` 10⁶ did not stop it, the walk is outside the flip cap); old source: tests/test_delaunay.py `test_recover_edges_duplicate_point_terminates` killed after 120 s, now 0.1 s; the m14 cause is fixed by B66 | provisional | — |
| M40 | A constraint through another vertex | a constraint a→b whose walk (the fan search at a, or the walk along a→b) meets a vertex w on it (an orient tie, \|orient\| ≤ 1e-12 \|b−a\|\|w−a\|, the predicates' tolerance; at the fan search only a w ahead of a) is split in place into (a, w), (w, b), directed as the parent, and the recovery runs again on the pieces (repeated until no piece meets a vertex); `sphere_delaunay` removes the land with the pieces (with a separate `coast`, the pieces of its edges) | delaunay.py `_recover` (`on_vertex`), `recover_edges(return_edges=)`, `sphere_delaunay` | a self-touching coast (L15; two land lobes meeting at one point, the touching vertex on the other lobe's segment) left that segment unrecovered, so `remove_land` flooded across it and kept both lobes as water (old: 1 of 5 constraints missing, 0 boundary edges, 2 land triangles kept, 3006 triangles = the whole sphere; now 6 boundary edges, 0 land triangles, 3004; tests/test_delaunay.py `test_self_touching_coast_split_at_the_touch`, both paths); meshes with no such tie are bit-identical (15 triangulations and methods at scale 4; docs/examples/fesom2_core2like.toml: 162 398 nodes, 312 234 triangles, gates 29/29, raw.npz and the FESOM2 files identical); the direct callers of `recover_edges` + `remove_land` (lfs.py `medial_axis`, repair.py `_m02_fill`, m10/layout.py, m14/continuation.py `cdt_local`) take `return_edges=True` and remove the land with the pieces too (2026-10-27; before, they recovered the pieces but `remove_land` got the unsplit edges and flooded the land: `medial_axis` on the touching ring kept 7652 = 2 N − 4 water triangles, the whole sphere, now 7650; `cdt_local` kept land-centred triangles, now none; tests/test_delaunay.py `test_medial_axis_self_touching_coast`, `test_cdt_local_self_touching_loop`); bit-identical where no tie occurs (medial axis of continent, seam_strait, seam_coast; m14_blocks continent, disc; m10_patch_layout continent; m14_blocks_rep continent; all at scale 4) | provisional | a w near but not on the segment (outside the tie): still a crossing the flips cannot remove, as before |

## 7. Competition protocol

| # | rule | value now | where | why | status | revisit when |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | Test cases | `uniform` 100 km; `disc` 10 km within 500 km of 30°W 40°N, linear to 50 km at 900 km; `band` 25 km south / 100 km north of the equator, g = 0.1; `continent` a 30° cap at 20°E 0°N cut by a 150 km channel (azimuth 45°) and a 300 km island at 60°E 10°N, 50 km with 15 km within 100 km of the channel, g = 0.1 | cases.py:13-57; domain.py:20-26 | together they test topology, an analytic ρ_c, a long transition, coast + island + strait | fixed | — |
| P2 | Channel resolution | 10 cells across the 150 km channel (≥ 7 required) | cases.py:13 | the required minimum across a strait (5, 7 or 10 cells) is not decided; 10 meets all three | provisional | — |
| P3 | Real coast case | 300 × 300 km Kattegat–Skagerrak, 5 km, g = 0.1; not built | — | a real coast | provisional | the first real-coast test |
| P4 | Scales | 4 (≈ 10⁴ nodes, quick), 1 (reference, 10⁵–2·10⁵), 0.3 (≈ 10⁶, scaling, only for the methods that pass) | cli.py:135 | — | fixed | — |
| P5 | Raw and smoothed | every run scored as the method made it and after the common smoothing (M1) | compete.py:232-286 | both the method's own output and the common post-processing are seen | fixed | — |
| P6 | Final run | one machine, serial, 16 CPUs on `shared`, `NUMBA_NUM_THREADS` = 16, timeout 1800 s | the final run's job script (not published) | earlier timings were noisy (up to 7 jobs shared one Mac) | fixed | — |
| P7 | Scaling run | scale 0.3; 64 CPUs and 60 GB on `shared` (96 CPUs / 90 GB for m07, m08); `NUMBA_NUM_THREADS` = 16; timeout 7200 s | the scaling run's job script (not published) | memory follows the CPU count on `shared` | provisional | — |
| P8 | Source of the results table | the competition's results table renders the final run's records | compete.py:320-345 | the table must show the final run, not an older record file | fixed | — |
| P9 | Gates counted per run | only the applicable gates (value not None), shown as passed / applicable | compete.py:217-220 | — | provisional | — |
| P10 | Ranking statistic | the number of failed gate families (gate name without the region suffix), N/A = not failed, over one fixed list | `gates_failed_families`, compete.py | passed / applicable orders 950 of 6048 method pairs differently | fixed | — |
| P11 | What counts as a pass | zero failed gates on all four cases at scale 1, raw; with the cluster gate (G13) no mixed method passes yet | — | — | provisional | the gates change |
| P12 | ρ_c per case | computed once at the reference scale and cached | compete.py:144-148 | ρ_c does not depend on the scale | fixed | — |
| P13 | Method inputs | (domain, h, opts) only, never the case name; the final run used each method's defaults (no `--opts`) | compete.py:191-195 | fairness | fixed | — |
| P14 | Time and memory | wall time of the method call in the child, Numba compilation included; peak RSS of the child (VmHWM on Linux); m14's own diagnostics left out of its time | compete.py:164-188, 232-244; bench.py | the cost of the method itself, comparable across methods | provisional | — |
| P15 | Reference baselines | external generators (JIGSAW, Gmsh, …) may run in their own env as `ref_*` rows scored with our measures; never part of meshom | methods/ref_jigsaw.py | a reference for our measures; meshom ships none of their code | fixed | — |
| P16 | Irregular node | interior node with valence ≠ 4; ≠ 6 on meshes with more triangles than quads | figures.py | — | provisional | the regular valence per node from its element types |
| P17 | Figure diagnostics | zooms of ≈ 30 × 30 local cells; defect kernel radius 3 cells; seam edge = \|ln s/h\| > 0.15; quads > 135° dotted | figures.py:52-57 | the gate values | provisional | the gates change |
| P18 | Acceptance of the triangle pipeline (m15_tri), stages S1–S5 | S1 fit: p99 ≤ 0.145 on all four cases, ratio p99 ≤ 1.15; S2 seeds: ≤ 0.165 (u/d/b), continent ≤ 0.20; S3: ≤ 0.13 (u/d/b), continent ≤ 0.15, ratio p99 ≤ 1.08, angles 35°–105°, irregular ≤ 2 %; S4: ≤ 0.12 (u/d/b), ≤ 0.13 continent (coast ≤ 0.15), angles 40°–100°, ratio p99 ≤ 1.08 and max ≤ 1.35, irregular ≤ 2 %, all triangle gates; S5: ≤ 0.10 (u/d) | m15 note | probes of the size fit and of the seeds (0.09–0.14 measured) | provisional | m15 is built |
| P19 | Seeds of m15 | a Fibonacci set snapped to water nodes; cap radius ≤ 0.4 rad and ≥ 500 nodes per cap (K = 30–1000 worked, 3000 did not) | m02_linf_frontal.py `seeds` (M27) | the seed scan (m15 note) | provisional | — |
| P20 | Population control of m15 | delete at ln(s/h) < −0.18, insert at > +0.22; 4–8 rounds of 10 "size" sweeps | popctl.py (M23, M24) | the population-control probe (m15 note) | provisional | — |
| P21 | size_fit energy of m15 | mean \|ln(s/h)\|^p, p = 6, h_e = √(h_i h_j); barrier μ Σ max(0, τ − det/det_ref)²; L-BFGS, 150 iterations per round, ≤ 3 rounds | size_fit.py (M28–M31) | the size-fit probe (m15 note) | provisional | — |
| P22 | Acceptance of m14_blocks_tri with the new fill | raw 18/18 (u/d/b) and 23/23 (continent); fill p99 ≤ 0.15, seam band ≤ 0.18 | — | — | provisional | — |
| P23 | Cost of m15 | band at scale 1 end to end ≤ 5 min on one core; deterministic | — | — | provisional | — |
| P24a | Scaling runs at 10⁶–10⁷ nodes (m15_tri, m01/m02 fit variants, m14 _rep/_mix at scale 0.3; m15_tri and m07_im on band at 0.2) | run on 2026-10-22 | the scaling run's job script (SCALE, CASES; not published) | long compute, run once | fixed | — |
| P24 | Scaling runs at 10⁶–10⁷ nodes | only as planned runs (P24a) | — | long compute | open | the next scaling run |
| P25 | Seam test family | 15 lakes registered in `CASES` (`seam_rot<θ>_k<k>`, θ ∈ {0, 15, 30, 45}°, k ∈ {0, 3, 10} per 100 cells; `seam_junction`; `seam_coast`; `seam_strait`): H0 = 25 km at scale 1, regions 40 H0, corridor half-width 3 H0 (the 6 h of m14's block gap) with 3 H0 trimmed at the coastal ends, a land ring 4 H0 beyond the farthest vertex, an inlet 8 H0 wide, world ocean h = H0 + 0.1 per metre beyond the ring (≤ 400 km), coast samples every 5 km with a fixed ±2 m jitter | cases_seams.py (`H0_M`, `CORRIDOR_HALF`, `TRIM`, `RING_H0`, `INLET_H0`, `G_OCEAN`, `H_FAR_M`, `JITTER_M`) | a coast is the only way (domain, h) can fix a lattice orientation; the ring and the inlet work round two kernel limits (the sphere Delaunay inside a hemisphere, the cross field on a second component); the jitter avoids exact in-circle ties (M13) | provisional | the kernel fixes for lakes alone (M36, F15), then no ring |
| P26 | Seam reference: the minimum dislocation count | D = \|B_x\| + \|B_y\| of the corridor's Burgers vector (Frank–Bilby) at exact size, the smallest over the quarter-turn renamings of side b's axes; gross charge ≥ 2 D; a junction core holds \|net\| ≥ 1 | quality/seams.py `dislocations`, `Core.net_ref` | dislocation theory (Frank–Bilby); a second derivation gives the same minimum (n_min = L(\|κ\| cos(θ/2) + 2 sin(θ/2)) along the bisector) | fixed | — |
| P27 | Seam measures | elements count by the centroid, nodes and edge midpoints by position in the trimmed corridor; boundary nodes and edges left out; the realised side lattices read from the quads 0–6 local cells beyond each corridor edge (4-fold mean edge angle, mean step per axis); "on the centreline" = within 1 h; irregular = valence ≠ 4 (≠ 6 at all-triangle nodes; the B53 angle sum at the nodes of a declared triangle zone) | quality/seams.py `seam_block`, `corridor_block`, `_side_lattice`, `SIDE_BAND_CELLS`, `CENTRE_CELLS` | the seam lives in the corridor; boundary effects are left out | provisional | — |
| P28 | Seam layout hint for m14 (a per-case `opts`) | `SEAM_HINTS[case]`: `seeds` one per region at the region's centre on its axis, 20 h (the arm's own h) beyond the region's near boundary: the bent bar's two arms (the corridor edge on the arm's axis, 3/cos(θ/2) H0 from the bend), the Y's three arms (their inner corners, 11.5 H0 from the centre), the strait's two basins (the mouth lines: seeds at (0, ±50) H0), the coast lake's region (8 H0 off the coast: (0, 28) H0); `grow_limit` = 20 h, so the L∞ growth square's near edge crosses the axis at that boundary and a block stays in its arm (since B67, 2026-10-25, the given seeds share a growth front by default and `grow_limit` is not applied: each seed's Voronoi cell keeps its block in its part); `align` = True (each block frame turned to the lake's B4 field); `strait_blocks` = False except on `seam_strait` (the inlet is a test artefact; a fixed block there cut the lake from its ocean and swung corridor gross per lake 25 → 54); merged under the caller's opts for m14's methods (not `_igm`, which ignores seeds) by `run_seams` (`--no-hint` turns it off) and recorded in the record's `opts`: the one exception to P13, seam cases only and visible in every record | cases_seams.py `SEAM_HINTS`, `HINT_GROW_H`, `_arm_seed`, `seam_opts`, `run_seams` | m14 alone puts one block across each lake (its frame from its first node's tangent basis), so no block–block seam is exercised; 20 h keeps a block inside its 40 H0 arm. Measured (m14_blocks and _mix, s1): 2 blocks on every bar, 3 on the Y, 2 on the strait, 1 on the coast lake; realised θ / design 0.72–0.76 at 15° and 30°, 0.21–0.22 at 45°, the Y's walls 0.70 / 0.99 / 1.18; the gap between the blocks 7.0–8.8 H0 at its narrowest on the bars (the corridor 6 H0; _mix strips it at θ ≤ 30°), 11.5–12.7 H0 on the Y (an open region); a seed square at the corridor edge where it meets the coast instead (no block in the corridor) left 7.8–18.3 H0 at the narrowest and no strip at 30° | provisional | a coast term replaces `align`; B4's +¼ cone at the convex corner of the 45° bend (the arm-A field is twisted, so `align` cannot realise 45°) |
| P29 | Seam table separate from the ranking | the record's `seam` block (quality/seams.py `seam_block`) is added by `compete._score` on every seam case (`cases_seams.SEAMS` has the case), so `meshom compete`, the rescoring of old records (which also keeps the record's `m14` summary) and the `_rep` variants carry it; the seam gates (G32–G37) never enter `gates_failed` or `family_table`; they are rendered as their own table, one row per case (`cases_seams.summary`: corridor gross / lake gross, m14's block count, the failed seam gates; "2 D" and "min"; cases passing) or one per corridor (`table`), between the `seam-table` markers of the target file | compete.py `_score`, `write_table` (markers as arguments); cases_seams.py `summary`, `table`, `write_table`, `SEAM_TABLE_BEGIN` / `SEAM_TABLE_END` | the seam gates are not calibrated and must not change the ranking; the 4-case ranking tables render byte-identical with the change | fixed | when the seam gates are calibrated (G0) |

## 8. FESOM output

| # | rule | value now | where | why | status | revisit when |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| O1 | Format | `[mesh] format` = `"fesom-c"` (default: the FESOM-C mixed format), `"fesom2"` (the FESOM2 triangle files; triangle meshes only, quads are not written) or `"fesom3"` (FESOM3, O24) | config.py (MeshCfg.format); run.py `run` (write stage); io/fesom.py `write_fesomc`, `write_fesom2` | FESOM3's mesh is expected to be FESOM2 + quads = FESOM-C, so the default stays; `fesom2` for the first FESOM2-style triangle mesh | open (FESOM3); `fesom2` provisional | FESOM3's format is known |
| O2 | `elem2d.out` columns | always 4; a triangle is `n1 n2 n3 n1` | io/fesom.py:53 | FESOM-C tests exactly n4 = n1; its MPI reader leaves node 4 unset in 3-column files | fixed | — |
| O3 | Element orientation in files | clockwise (east = x, north = y): the internal CCW order reversed | io/fesom.py:53, 68 | FESOM-C and FESOM2 make elements clockwise; writing clockwise avoids their swaps and log floods | fixed | — |
| O4 | Element order | triangles first, then quads | io/fesom.py:53 | FESOM-C has no order requirement | provisional | — |
| O5 | `nod2d.out` | count line, then `id lon lat flag`, id = line number 1..N, `%15.10f` | io/fesom.py:15, 40-44 | the MPI reader uses the line order, the serial reader stores by id | fixed | — |
| O6 | Node flag | 1 on topological boundary nodes, 0 elsewhere; never 2 yet | run.py:224-227 | FESOM-C tests only flag 2 (open boundary); 0 / 1 is the FESOM1.4 convention | fixed | regional mode: flag 2 on open-boundary nodes, both end nodes included |
| O7 | Node depth in the files (`depth.out` of FESOM-C, `aux3d.out` of FESOM2) | the bathymetry stage's depth (O16–O19); without `[bathymetry] source` the placeholder 4400 m | `PLACEHOLDER_DEPTH_M`, run.py; io/fesom.py | the wet mean over the node's cell (O16) | provisional | — |
| O8 | FESOM-C `aux3d.out` | 5 levels 0..4, then −4400 per node (FESOM2: O11, O19) | io/fesom.py:56-58 | the FESOM-C partitioner needs nl ≥ 5 | provisional | the depth stage |
| O9 | Coordinates in the files | geographic lon-lat, not rotated | io/fesom.py:40-44; config.md:174 | FESOM2 files hold geographic coordinates and the model rotates on read (`force_rotation`); rotation is dead code in FESOM-C | fixed | FESOM3 wants rotated files |
| O10 | Rotation | Euler angles 50° / 15° / −90° (FESOM2's r2g) available, not applied; the rotated poles must fall on land with a margin (north over Greenland) | geo.py:57-60 | as in FESOM2 | provisional | FESOM3's rotation is known |
| O11 | FESOM2 writer (triangle meshes only) | 3 columns, clockwise; `aux3d.out` = nl, the nl levels and the N node depths, all written negative down (`0.0000`, `-5.0000`, …), as CORE2's own file; flag 1 on boundary nodes | io/fesom.py `write_fesom2`; reader `read_fesom`, `read_aux3d` | FESOM2 and its partitioner read both signs (positive levels and depths are turned negative); fesom2_mesh_gen (D. Sein, AWI) writes the depths positive, CORE2 negative (`/pool/data/AWICM/FESOM2/MESHES_FESOM2.1/core2/aux3d.out`). Orientation: FESOM2 makes every triangle clockwise when it reads it, so meshom writes them clockwise (O3) | fixed | — |
| O12 | Strictly convex quads, no bow-ties | ensured by the SJ gate (G6) | — | FESOM-C's own check misses some bow-ties | fixed | — |
| O13 | Node numbering | after a cut, boundary loops first; no Hilbert renumbering yet; renumbering invalidates per-node files (m2.out, rivers.dat, …) | m14/cut.py:151-169 | FESOM-C matches per-node inputs by node order (m2.out: one row per open-boundary node in ascending node index) | provisional | regional mode; memory locality (a Hilbert order) |
| O14 | The output format itself | adjustable at any time, not important now | — | a writer is cheap to change | fixed | — |
| O15 | Provenance next to every mesh | `config.resolved.toml`, `stages.json` (time, memory, machine, commit), `quality.json` | run.py:14-17; config.md:166-175 | every mesh can be traced to its config, code and machine | fixed | — |
| O16 | Depth per node | mean of the grid's **ocean** cells (elevation < 0) in a lon-lat box of half-width r = half the node's mean edge length (r / cos(lat) east-west): about the node's control cell (box area h², median-dual cell 0.87 h²); land cells left out | bathymetry.py `node_depth`, `box_means`; `[bathymetry] depth_at = "node"` | FESOM2 sets each element's depth from its 3 nodes (mean, `which_depth_n2e`), so a node stands for its cell's water; the same box mean of ocean pixels as in fesom2_mesh_gen (D. Sein, AWI); the nearest cell would sample one 450 m pixel for a 25–110 km cell | provisional | depth smoothing or an element-depth file (`depth@elem.out`) is wanted |
| O17 | Nodes without an ocean cell in their box | the 1/edge-length-weighted mean of the known neighbours, ring by ring; a component without any known node gets `min_depth_m` | bathymetry.py `fill_from_neighbours` | GSHHS water where GEBCO has land (lagoons, the coastline mismatch): 5 of 162 557 nodes in fesom2_core2like; the same ring extrapolation as in fesom2_mesh_gen | provisional | — |

## 10. Levante and process rules that constrain results

| # | rule | value now | where | why | status | revisit when |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| L1 | Compute runs | as Slurm jobs; a login node only for tiny single-process tasks (one test file of seconds) | scripts/run_example.sbatch, scripts/on_node.sh | login nodes are shared | fixed | — |
| L2 | VTK / Mesa rendering | not a tiny task: `LP_NUM_THREADS=1` or a compute node | — | 1184 % CPU on the login node | fixed | — |
| L3 | Memory on `shared` | ≤ 0.9 GB × CPUs (940 MB per CPU), otherwise Slurm raises the CPU count | scripts/on_node.sh | billing | fixed | — |
| L4 | Partitions | `shared` (7 days, a per-user CPU cap); `interactive` (1940 MB per CPU, 12 h: memory-heavy jobs and whenever `shared` shows `QOSMaxCpuPerUserLimit`); `compute` (whole nodes, 8 h, long queue: only the big runs) | — | Levante's partitions and their limits | fixed | — |
| L5 | Long compute runs | planned one by one, never started automatically | — | the compute budget | fixed | — |
| L6 | Code of a second checkout in runs | `PYTHONPATH=$PWD python -m meshom.cli ...`, never the `meshom` console script | scripts/run_example.sbatch | the editable install maps `meshom` to the checkout it was installed from | fixed | — |
| L7 | Code recorded with a result | `commit` = the runner's HEAD; the worker's code is not recorded, nor a dirty flag or a source hash | compete.py:266-267; bench.py | a known gap: records made from uncommitted code name commits that do not contain their method | provisional | a record must name the exact code of its method |
| L8 | Threads for comparable timings | `NUMBA_NUM_THREADS` = 16 in the final and the scaling run | the job scripts of those runs (not published) | — | provisional | — |
| L9 | Reproducibility across machines | results reproduce per platform, not across (m02 band s4: 27 137 nodes on the Mac, 26 872 on Levante) | — | — | fixed | a cross-platform requirement appears |
| L10 | Other machines and repositories | meshom runs on one machine and sends nothing out; external repositories are only read, never changed | — | — | fixed | — |
| L11 | Transient files | in `.tmp/` of the repository, never in system temp dirs | — | — | fixed | — |
| L12 | GPU | Levante A100 (fp64) if its GPUs are available, else another server; GPU only after the structure works on CPU | — | fp64 needs a data-centre GPU; the structure comes first | fixed | — |
| L13 | Exploration code (the competing methods; status "exploration" in a row) | minimal tests while methods are compared; a method chosen for production gets the full test set | — | many methods are tried, few are kept | fixed | the chosen methods are hardened |
| L14 | What gets fixed | visible failures and stated rules without a test; a failure that may surface some day is noted, not fixed at once | — | — | fixed | — |
| L15 | Input scope | no defences against user mistakes; what real data holds (tiny islands, lakes, self-touching coast polygons, the antimeridian, Antarctica and the poles, one-cell-wide straits) must work | — | real data must work; a config's mistakes are the user's | fixed | — |

## 11. Local repair (`meshom/repair.py`)

`DEFAULTS`, `repair.py`; variants `methods/rep_variants.py`; CLI `meshom repair`; note `docs/methods/repair.md`
(mechanism, index budget, results). Added on 2026-10-22; line numbers are not given because the module is new: the
option names are the handles.

| # | rule | value now | where | why | status | revisit when |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | Defect element | touches an interior node with valence ≠ 4 (≠ 6 where all its elements are triangles); quad θmax > 135°; any triangle of a quad-dominant mesh; triangle failing a hard angle gate; either side of an edge with \|ln s/h\| > 0.15 | `bad_quad_deg`, `bad_edge`; `_Glob` | the target gates G2, G9 and the irregular-node rule P16 | provisional | the gates change |
| R2 | Coast row | elements touching a coast node (the `_coast` subset of G17) never seed a cluster and are never part of a cavity: coast nodes are never moved or removed, the coastal size fidelity stays exactly as it was and no size jump is added next to the coast row (R9) | `_Glob.coast_row`, `allowed`; `_build_cavity` | coast nodes stay fixed; a first run that let cavities take the coast row made m14 continent fail its coastal size gate (24/24 → 25/26) | fixed | coast nodes may slide (C21) |
| R3 | Cluster | edge-connected components of the defect set dilated by one element; components above 100 elements cut into windows of ≤ 100 (breadth-first balls from the worst element left) | `window` = 100 | the components are whole dislocation walls: 1176 elements (m02 uniform s1), 22 924 (m14 band s1 ramp); arbitrary, first guess for the size | provisional | — |
| R4 | Ranking and skipping | score = w_irr (gross − \|net\|) + w_elem (bad elements) + w_edge (bad edges); ties by centroid z, y, x; score ≤ 0 = the charge is needed, window left alone | `_Glob.clusters` | what a repair should remove: irregular nodes above the local need, bad angles, bad edges | provisional | — |
| R5 | Ring schedule | rings 2, then 4, then 8 (`rings` doubled up to `rings_max`); every window with a positive score is tried again with the larger cavity; `grow_failed_only` limits the 4- and 8-ring stages to windows that failed with fewer rings | `rings` = 2, `rings_max` = 8, `grow_failed_only` = False | a larger remesh zone is acceptable, so it grows 2 → 4 → 8; retrying all windows: m02 disc s1 \|ln s/h\| p99 0.148 (18/18) vs 0.170 (16/18) with failed windows only, m14 band gross 4416 vs 4486, at 2× the time | provisional | most of the gross gain comes at 2 rings (repair note) |
| R6 | Block rows a cavity may take | rows 0–1 from the original block outline (node rings); deeper block elements never | `block_rows` = 2 | the first block rows may be taken, so a strip can absorb a row-count mismatch | provisional | — |
| R7 | Cavity shape | pinch nodes get their whole fan; holes filled (breadth-first, capped at 4\|C\| + 100 elements); 2 sweeps adding the fans of concave loop nodes (right turn > 45°); one outer loop (islands allowed); ≤ 3000 elements | `convexify` = 2, `max_cavity` = 3000 | manifold exact loops for the fills; squarer cavities for Coons; arbitrary, first guess for the counts | provisional | — |
| R8 | Badness | 2 × gross charge + 2 × bad elements + 1 × bad edges (\|ln s/h\| > 0.15 on cavity edges, element size ratio > 1.15 across edges with a cavity element) | `w_irr`, `w_elem`, `w_edge`, `bad_ratio` | integers, so every accepted patch lowers an integer (termination); arbitrary, first guess for the weights | provisional | FESOM3 noise vs irregular nodes and size errors is known |
| R9 | Acceptance | strictly lower badness; no inverted element; no hard gate (G1, G3, G5, G6, G8, G10, G11, non-manifold edges) with more violations in the cavity + first ring than before; neither the gross charge nor the count of bad elements (quads > 135°, triangles failing an angle gate) above the old one; no more edges with element size ratio > 1.15 next to the coast row; the whole mesh's count of such edges within a budget (R18); net charge equal; patch boundary = the loops, consistent orientation, same Euler characteristic | `_accept`, `gross_slack` = 0, `hard`, `ratio_guard` = "budget"; `_Cav.valid` | a patch strictly improves, inverts no element, adds no gate violation and keeps the Euler sum; the counts are the local form of "no gate violation that was not there" for the target gates G2 and G7: trading them let m14 continent lose its coastal size gate, m14 / m03 disc reach a 149° quad and m14 uniform s4 lose the ratio gate (16/18 → 14/18) in the first runs | provisional | — |
| R18 | Size-jump budget | edges with element size ratio > 1.15 may grow in a cavity while the whole mesh stays below 0.9 % of its interior edges (the p99 gate's 1 % with a 10 % margin) when the input passes the gate, and may not grow at all when it fails; patches over the budget are rejected in rank order | `ratio_share` = 0.009; `ratio_guard` "budget" ("count": never more in a cavity) | a per-cavity "never more" halves the gains (m14 band gross 5442 vs 4416, m02 disc 16/18 vs 18/18); the budget keeps m14 uniform s4 at ratio p99 1.146 (16/18 kept) | provisional | the ratio gate changes |
| R10 | Candidates | "ops", "coons", "m02" in this order, the best badness wins, ties to the earlier; "m02_tri" available but off | `candidates` (CLI: `--opts '{"candidates": [...]}'`, any subset in the order tried; recorded in `opts` of the CLI record) | m02_tri: 0 of 1291 valid patches accepted on m02 uniform s1 (triangles count as defects in a quad-dominant mesh) | provisional | triangle meshes get repaired |
| R11 | Topology operations | edge rotation, pentagon re-split, triangle pair merge or flip, diagonal collapse, doublet removal; greedy on the gross charge; ≤ 100 operations; depth-2 lookahead over ≤ 40 zero-cost first moves; then 20 "size" + 3 "angle" sweeps, loop fixed | `ops_max`, `lookahead` = 40, `smooth_size`, `smooth_angle` | lookahead 0 / 8 / 16 / 40 on m02 uniform s1: gross 772 / 758 / 744 / 730, +25 s | provisional | — |
| R12 | Coons corners and strip | corner = left turn > 45°; exactly 4, no right turn < −45°; equal opposite sides: grid; one pair unequal: strip, transitions spread over the middle rows | `corner_deg` = 45, `strip` = True | a quad corner of a regular loop turns 90°; arbitrary, first guess for the tolerance | provisional | — |
| R13 | m02 fill size | only cavities ≤ 800 elements | `m02_max` = 800 | cost (field + packing per cavity); arbitrary, first guess | provisional | — |
| R14 | Rounds and failure memory | ≤ 3 rounds of the ring stages, stop when a round accepts nothing; a failed window is retried only when one of its nodes changed | `max_rounds` = 3 | bounded work; a window is retried only when something around it changed | provisional | — |
| R15 | Parallel evaluation | the cavities of a wave (an independent set in rank order) are evaluated by a pool of spawned processes; the result does not depend on the worker count | `workers` = 0 (the CPUs available, ≤ 16) | m02 band s1 would exceed the 1800 s method timeout serially; test `test_deterministic` | provisional | — |
| R16 | Hard-gate values in the repair | copies of `DEFAULT_GATES` (150°, 0.3, 120°, 20°, 2.0, 2, 32); the newer gates are not checked per cavity: Euler and pinches are kept by the patch topology check (boundary = the loops), boundary edges cannot change (the coast row is never touched), triangle clusters are not checked (measured after the merge: every cluster of ≥ 3 outside the coast row removed, m14 15 → 0 on band) | `hard` | the repair was written before those gates existed | provisional | read them from the config; add `tri_cluster_max` to the per-cavity check |
| R17 | One zone by hand (`repair_region`) | elements with the centroid inside the polygon + `rings` rings; coast-row elements allowed (coast nodes stay loop nodes, fixed); same acceptance; `force` applies the best valid patch (topology, nothing inverted, net kept) without improvement | `region_coast_row` = True, `force` = False | the UI's "select a zone and remesh": the user decides where | provisional | the UI is built |
| R19 | `meshom repair` on a run folder | without `--case`: domain and h from the run's `config.resolved.toml` (else `config.toml`) as `meshom run` builds them (`coast_of`, `size_field`; `[coast] treatment = "cut"`: the mesh's own boundary loops; source `none`: the closed sphere), the config's gates; `--mesh` (default `raw.npz`) and the output stem `--out` (default `repaired`: `<NAME>.npz`, `<NAME>.json`) relative to RUN_DIR; the record holds `input`, `polygon`, `opts` (resolved), `before` / `after` (`compete._score` of the whole mesh), `repair` (the info record) and, for a zone, `region` before / after (R21); `--case` keeps the competition case | cli.py `_repair`; docs/methods/repair.md "CLI" | the UI repairs its run folders and chains repairs; `--out` was a directory and the record `repair.json`, the name the `_rep` variants write into a compete run directory | provisional | — |
| R20 | Block mask across repairs | the output npz carries `is_block` (the repair's `is_block_after`, triangles first) when the input had blocks; an input npz with `is_block` uses it, else `m14.json`'s block-quad prefix for `raw.npz` / `smooth.npz` (not after a `meshom run` land cut, which renumbers the elements); the deep rows (R6) are counted from the current block outline, so where a repair replaced outer block rows the next one in a chain may take `block_rows` more | cli.py `_repair`; compete.py `_save(**arrays)`; repair.py `repair_region` `is_block_after` | chained repairs from the UI; test `test_cli_chain_m14_blocks` | provisional | long chains eat into the blocks |
| R21 | Zone record | `region_summary`: the elements with the centroid inside the polygon (repair_region's core, without its rings), their compute_quality / size_fidelity statistics (the `_strait` subset with the polygon as the corridor), n3 / n5 / n_other of the interior nodes inside, the gates on the zone (the triangle target gate by the whole mesh's quad count, as in `_score`) | repair.py `region_summary`, `polygon_inside` | the UI shows before / after of the touched zone | provisional | — |
| R22 | The repair under a size tolerance | `m14_blocks_rep` with `size_tolerance` t (0.20 by default, G38; 0 = the plain repair): m14 runs without the smooth fit, the repair's `bad_edge` = max(t, 0.15) (unless the repair options give one), then `size_fit.smooth_fit` at t on the repaired mesh (coast and the remaining block nodes fixed; record `tol_fit_final.json`) | rep_variants.py `m14_blocks_rep` | the fit inside the stitch followed by the repair with its bad edge at t left triangle clusters of 3 the repair no longer removed (disc s1 at 0.10 / 0.20 / 0.30, uniform at 0.10, CORE2-like at all three; 51 operations accepted against 175 on uniform at 0.10) and raised the CORE2-like ratio to 1.150 at 0.30; fit last: CORE2-like 39 / 39 / 38 of 39 at 0.10 / 0.20 / 0.30, every scale-1 gate at 0.20 and 0.30 | provisional | — |
| R23 | Declared triangle zones in the repair | triangles of `Mesh.tri_zone` are never defects, never in a cluster (not even by the dilation) and never in a cavity (`repair`, and `repair_region`); the returned mesh carries the zone mask (new elements outside it), through the coast stage too | repair.py `_Glob.zone`, `repair`, `repair_region` | a zone is a method's intentional triangle fill (G13); before 2026-10-27 the repair dropped the mask (even with no candidate accepted: tri_cluster_max 0 → 8, and the optimisation then merged all 8 zone triangles) and its "ops" candidate replaced zone triangles by quads (test `tests/test_repair.py::test_tri_zone_kept`). A cavity around a zone would have the zone as a hole, which the closure fills, so such a cavity fails ("coast_or_block") and the zone's rim is left as it is, as with deep block rows (R6). Meshes without a zone are unchanged | provisional | the rim of a zone needs repair |
| R24 | Frozen fixed blocks in `m14_blocks_rep` | the cells of m14's fixed blocks whose kind is in the option `freeze_blocks` (default `["hand"]`: the hand-placed blocks, B60; `"strait"` (B59) and `"band"` (C36) possible, `[]` = none) are frozen in every stage after m14: in the repair and its coast stage held as R23's zones (never defects, never in a cluster or a cavity: their nodes are loop nodes, never moved), never split by the coast fix (B64; their nodes fixed there, so the cluster break never takes them), their nodes fixed in the final fit (R22), and in the optimisation stage (Z1) never in an operation's removed set, their nodes fixed; the cells next to them are still repaired; frozen cells are found on each later mesh by their node positions (`cells_on`, within 1e-9 of the unit sphere) | rep_variants.py `FREEZE_KINDS`, `frozen_cells`, `cells_on`, `m14_blocks_rep`; repair.py `repair(frozen=)`, `_Glob.hold`; m14/coastfix.py `coast_fix(frozen=)`, `split_coast_quads(frozen=)`; optimize.py `optimize(frozen=)`, `_Work.patch_ok`; m14_blocks.py `CUT_FIXED` (`fixed_id`, `fixed_kind`), `optimize_stage(frozen=)`; m14/stitch.py `fixed_kind` | a drawn block stays as drawn. Before: under a cut (the closed sphere, banks not coast nodes) the repair took the whole continent channel by hand (cols 3): 0 of 51 cells kept at scale 4 (nodes moved 3–43 km), 0 of 24 at scale 8 (16–92 km), conforming at scale 8 6 of 24 changed (4 nodes moved 50 km). Now 51 of 51 and 24 of 24, no node moved, also through the land cut and the run's own optimisation stage at scale 4 (cut gates 35/39 as before and as `m14_blocks`; repair 81 accepted against 92, triangles 262 against 244). Without hand blocks every mesh is bit for bit the same: glob100 and CORE2-like conforming (`glob100_coast_nobands_v2`, `core2_mixed_coast_nobands`, raw.npz identical) and the continent case cut / conforming at scales 2 and 4. Strait blocks not frozen by default: freezing them kept the continent channel's strait block whole at scale 2 (1110 of 1110 against 1094, 12 nodes moved ≤ 2.9 km) at 37/39 gates either way, but on the production meshes it made things a little worse at 39/39: glob100 triangles 1471 → 1477, n3 / n5 146 / 1383 → 150 / 1394, quad edge ratio max 1.50 → 1.85; CORE2-like triangles 9064 → 9118, n3 / n5 1017 / 8709 → 1034 / 8784, quad edge ratio max 1.99 → 2.37. Tests: tests/test_frozen_blocks.py (without the change: 0 of 24 under a cut, 18 of 24 conforming at scale 8, the new keywords missing) | provisional | a cavity that would surround a frozen block fails (the block is not an island hole), as with R23's zones; `meshom repair` / `repair_region` on a saved run know no frozen cells; whether strait blocks should be frozen after all |

## 12. Optimisation stage (`meshom/optimize.py`)

`DEFAULTS`, `optimize.py`; m14 option `optimize` (on); config `[mesh] optimize`, `optimize_opts`; CLI `meshom
optimize`. Added on 2026-10-25 as a method-independent post-stage (quality over speed), on by default for m14. The
option names are the handles.

| # | rule | value now | where | why | status | revisit when |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Z1 | The stage | **on by default for every m14 method** (`m14_blocks` and every registered variant, `m14_blocks_rep` included) as the method's own last stage (after `_cut`'s cut; `m14_blocks_rep`: after its repair, coast stage and fit, with m14 run without it), at the method's size tolerance, record `info["optimize"]` / `<run dir>/optimize.json`, so `meshom run`, `meshom compete` and `cases_seams` give the same mesh; off for the other methods. `[mesh] optimize` unset = the method's default (`methods.own_optimize`), true / false (1 / 0) forced; a method without its own stage, and an m14 method under a `meshom run` land cut (treatment "cut": the method then runs without it), get it from the run after the method (and the smoothing) on the final mesh, saved as `optimized.npz`, scored as `quality.json` "optimized", and the mesh of the later stages (bathymetry, write, straits); never twice; `meshom optimize DIR` the same on a saved mesh (`<NAME>.npz`, `<NAME>.json`, and the record `<NAME>` of a run's `quality.json`) | m14 option `optimize` = True, `optimize_opts` (m14_blocks.py `DEFAULTS`, `optimize_stage`; rep_variants.py); `[mesh] optimize` = unset, `optimize_opts`; run.py; cli.py `_optimize` | a post-stage after the generation that improves any mesh; on by default for the m14 methods, as their last stage (2026-10-25); the land cut renumbers the closed sphere's elements (m14's `CUT_FIXED` record indexes them), so there the run's stage follows the cut. Measured (scale-1 raw meshes): `tri_cluster_max` passes on all 32 meshes of m14_blocks, `_mix`, `_rep`, `_igm`, `_cut`, `_tri`, m07, m08 × uniform / disc / band / continent (12 of them failed it), on the global conforming `m14_blocks_rep` (38 → 39 of 39, +15 s) and on `m14_blocks_rep` continent s0.3 (44 → 45 of 45, 56 s at 2.2 M nodes); no gate lost anywhere; Euler, boundary and inverted counts kept. With the default on: competition s1 raw: failed families m14_blocks 4 → 0, `_cut` 4 → 0, `_mix` 1 → 0, `_igm` 4 → 1 (uniform size p99 0.26, untouched by the local stage), `_tri` 0 → 0, `_rep` 0 → 0; smoothed: m14_blocks / `_mix` / `_rep` 1 (continent size p99), `_cut` / `_tri` 0, `_igm` 4; wall time at s1 +0–68 % (m14_blocks 16 → 22 s uniform, 163 → 185 s band; `_igm` band 120 → 202 s the most; `_rep` continent 175 → 229 s, its other cases and `_tri` within ±1 %); seam lakes pass 8 / 5 / 0 / 14 of 15 (m14_blocks / `_mix` / `_tri` / `_rep`; m02 4), against 6 / 5 / 0 / 8 for older records re-gated with G33 (other code changed in between too) | provisional | the other methods' defaults (m07 / m08 with `fit = "all"`) |
| Z2 | Steps and order | flips → (pair merges, cluster breaking, valence) repeated → flips again → fit; the topology steps only on meshes with quads (a triangle mesh gets flips and the fit) | `steps` = flip, merge, cluster, valence, fit | merges first: they are cheap and remove most triangle pairs, so the cluster step sees only the hard cases; flips keep the triangle set (clusters unchanged), so they may come first and last | provisional | — |
| Z3 | Rounds | the topology steps repeat while a round accepts something, at most 3 rounds | `passes` = 3 | the measured runs stop after 2–3 rounds | provisional | — |
| Z4 | Fixed parts | nodes of the topological boundary, nodes with node_flag ≠ 0 and the caller's are never moved or removed, boundary edges never change; triangles of a declared zone (`tri_zone`) take part only in flips among themselves | `_Work.fixed`, `zone` | boundary and coast nodes stay fixed; the zone is a method's intended triangle fill | fixed | coast nodes may slide (C21) |
| Z5 | Flip rule | an interior triangle-triangle edge flips when the convex quad's other diagonal lowers the pair's largest angle (by > 1e-9 rad) and keeps its smallest angle ≥ min(20°, the old smallest); never a triangle with two boundary edges, no node above 32 elements, an interior node keeps ≥ 3; ≤ 50 passes | `tri_min` = 20, `flip_passes` = 50; `_angle_flips` | the gated triangle quantities: the largest angle (G3, target G4), the smallest (G5, 20°), boundary edges (G13), elements per node (G11); the pair's largest angle falls strictly, so the passes end | provisional | — |
| Z6 | Patch validity | a replacement keeps the patch's boundary half-edges, has no repeated node in a cell or directed half-edge, the same Euler characteristic, every new interior edge new to the mesh, and removes only free interior nodes whose whole star it replaces; the net charge is kept (a check) | `_Work.patch_ok`, `accept` | no inverted element, Euler and boundary unchanged; the checks of every result: `info["checks"]` (Euler sum and χ, boundary edge set, inverted count) | fixed | — |
| Z7 | Quads an operation makes | every corner within [45°, 135°] for a pair merge (cost < 0.5, greedy cheapest first); for the other operations no quad above 135° more than before (bad elements) | `quad_max` = 135 | the quad target gate G2 (135° at p99.9); new quads should not use its slack | provisional | the gate changes |
| Z8 | Acceptance | measured on the nodes of the patch plus the free nodes within 1 ring, with their elements and first ring (repair._measure): nothing inverted, no hard-gate count (G1, G3, G5, G6, G8, G10, G11, non-manifold) up, no element with two boundary edges more, no bad element (quad > 135°, triangle failing a hard angle) more, no size jump next to the coast row more, no bad edge (\|ln s/h\| > tol) or size jump (ratio > 1.15) more in the region or its _coast3 part (but Z11); then merge: fewer triangles, gross charge not up; cluster: fewer triangles in clusters ≥ 3, gross charge not up (slack 0); valence: gross charge down by ≥ 2; best candidate: fewest cluster triangles, then bad elements, then bad edges + size jumps, then the smallest worst corner deviation | `accept`, `_rank`, `gross_slack` = 0, `bad_ratio` = 1.15 | the local form of "no gate lost" (as R9); the gross rule is checked first from the topology (exact, test `test_gross_change_from_topology_is_exact`) | provisional | — |
| Z9 | Candidates of a cluster | unions of connected sets of 2–3 elements with a cluster triangle, the cluster itself (≤ 4 triangles) alone and with one neighbour, the stars of free interior nodes at the cluster (≤ 8 elements): simple polygons of ≤ 8 nodes re-split in every way into quads and n mod 2 triangles; evaluated as they are, then (none accepted) after relaxing the free nodes within 1 ring (20 "size" + 3 "angle" sweeps of meshom.smooth, R11's values) | `cluster_sets` = 3, `poly_max` = 8, `relax_rings` = 1, `relax_size` = 20, `relax_angle` = 3; clusters of more than `cluster_size_max` = 12 triangles are skipped (a triangle region: the merges are the tool, one operation per pass would not reach it; counted) | the leftover clusters of the measured meshes are 3–4 interior triangles whose pair merges give 140–178° (probe 2026-10-25): they need a neighbour quad in the polygon or a node removed, and a relaxation | provisional | — |
| Z10 | Valence operations | at interior free nodes whose elements are all quads and whose valence ≠ 4: removal of a valence-2 or -3 node (star re-split), the diagonal collapse of a quad between two valence-3 nodes whose neighbours share exactly the quad's other corners, the edge rotation of two quads (hexagon re-split); kept when the gross charge falls by ≥ 2 | `valence`; Z8 | valence fixes only where they pay (3–5 pairs that cancel); a single 3–5 pair carries a Burgers vector and can only move (repair note, `docs/methods/repair.md`), so a lone glide is never taken | provisional | a 2-step lookahead (glide + cancel) as R11 |
| Z11 | Budget of a cluster operation | a cluster operation may add up to 4 bad edges and 4 size jumps (each also in the _coast3 subset) while the whole mesh and its _coast3 subset keep below 0.9 % of their edges (the input's count when it is already above) | `budget_share` = 0.009, `budget_op` = 4 | the gates are p99 (1 %, R18's 10 % margin); strict counts left the hardest clusters (disc, band, continent of m14_blocks s1) unbroken: their best candidates add 1–7 size jumps | provisional | the ratio or size gate changes |
| Z12 | Fit | size_fit.smooth_fit at the run's tolerance (else 0.15) on the free nodes within 3 rings of every node an operation touched; kept unless the region's measure is worse (inverted, hard counts, bad elements, bad edges + size jumps) | `fit` = "local" ("all", "off"), `fit_rings` = 3, `fit_opts` | the methods already ran their own fit (m14, m15); the stage only relaxes what it changed (on the submesh of the elements at the free nodes' elements: the same energy, 48.6 → 2.9 s at 2.2 M nodes); "all" at the size gate 0.15 takes m07 and m08 continent s1 from 43 to 45 of 45 (their coast size gates; ratio p99 1.05 → 1.02, size p99 0.105 → 0.140; 130–140 s); JAX / GPU for the fit at 10⁶–10⁷ nodes is later (the Numba fit is the CPU path) | provisional | a mesh of 10⁶–10⁷ nodes |
| Z13 | Fit phase of a cluster | a cluster with no accepted candidate as is or relaxed: its 8 best relaxed near misses (accepted but for the size-jump / bad-edge counts of Z8 / Z11) are evaluated again with the free nodes moved by size_fit.smooth_fit at the tolerance (on the submesh of their elements, the same energy as on the whole mesh) instead of the size-blind relaxation | `fit_try` = 8; `_Work.fit` | m14_blocks_igm band s1 sits at its bad-edge budget (8259 = 1.0 % of the edges beyond 0.20; size p99 0.1995): its 7 leftover clusters had candidates adding 1–2 edges beyond 0.20 after the size-blind relaxation; with the fit phase all 7 break (5 by the fit), bad edges 8259 → 8257, 20/21 → 21/21 | provisional | — |
## 13. Licence and sources

| # | rule | value now | where | why | status | revisit when |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| N1 | Licence of the repository | GNU GPL v3 or later (SPDX `GPL-3.0-or-later`) for every file | `LICENSE`, `NOTICE`, `pyproject.toml`, the SPDX line of `meshom/orca/*.py` | `meshom/orca/generator.py` is a close port of G. Madec's ORCA grid generator, which NEMO distributes under the CeCILL licence; CeCILL v2 allows a derived work to be redistributed under the GNU GPL, so one licence covers the whole repository | fixed | — |
| N2 | Code and text of others | none is copied in: a method taken from another tool or a paper is written in meshom's own code and credited once where it lives (the paper, or the repository's URL); the ORCA generator port keeps its original's attribution (`NOTICE`, the header of `generator.py`); external generators (JIGSAW, Gmsh) run only as optional reference baselines and are not shipped | `NOTICE`; the module docstrings | the licences of other code stay untouched when none of it is shipped | fixed | a new third-party source: note its licence here when it is first used |
